The save lives in the kin's folder as before; `HEARTHKIN_TFF_PATH` is unchanged.
- **`tff` is more forgiving about how a kin phrases park commands** (improvement in the game repo it bridges to). A small local model that says "care for outdoor room Outdoor 2" or "care for Juna and Otiscuit in Indoor 1" now resolves to the right room instead of bouncing off exact-match. The game's headless layer is also now playable by a person at a terminal (`python tff_play.py`).
- **The "couldn't find the game" message now points to the download.** When a kin reaches for `tff` (or any future game) and the game folder isn't where expected, the error names the public repo to get a copy from — so a new operator without the game knows where to look, instead of just being told to set a path to a thing they don't have. Carried per-game on `GameHost` (`repo_url`); `tff` points at the now-public [Time for Family](https://github.com/glasswings-lang/time-for-family) repo.

### Fixed

- **`run_tool_loop` no longer leaks raw `<tool_call>` markup on a bail.** When the loop stops early (a stuck repeat, or the iteration cap), its final no-tools turn could surface a model's content-form tool call (`<tool_call><function=…>`) as visible text. Both bail paths now strip those markers the way the normal path does.
- **The `tff` tool steers the model to actually call it, not narrate the action.** A relational kin tended to roleplay park actions in asterisks (`*digs*`, `*pets the cats*`) instead of issuing the call, so nothing happened; the tool description now says park actions only take effect on a real call.

## v0.7.0 — 2026-06-28

### Added — a kin can play a game (creature_park / Time for Family)

- **New `creature_park` tool: a kin plays its own cozy creature-park text game.** One plain-English command per turn (`look`, `adopt cat`, `dig 50`, `build indoor`, `breed`, `care for`…), replies in plain words; each kin keeps its own private park (separate save), never the human's. It's a thin bridge to the headless layer of **Time for Family**, a wx-free Python game; the game folder is found at call time (env var → `~/.hearthkin/creature_park_path.txt` → conventional spots), with no path baked into the shippable tool, and a clear "point me at the game" message if it's missing. Enable it per-kin in Settings → Tools; it sits in the Telegram **write** tier.
- **`GameHost` — reusable plumbing so the *next* game tool is a ~10-line drop-in.** The locate-the-game / per-kin-save / run-a-command machinery is factored into `tools/_game_host.py`; `creature_park` is the template. Adding a second game is: a tiny tool file, two registry lines, one bucket line, a checkbox — and a new **bucket-coverage test** fails loudly if a tool is ever left out of a Telegram bucket (the way creature_park itself was briefly invisible on Telegram).

### Fixed — small / local models fumbling tools (a robustness sweep)

- **`edit_file` heals the usual near-misses.** A small model often gets `old_string` *almost* right — it pastes in read_file's `[read_file: …]` footer, guesses a trailing blank line, gets the indentation off, or mismatches CRLF vs LF — and the edit used to fail hard, after which the model spiraled into an empty reply. Now each of those is healed as a fallback *after* the exact match misses, on a unique match only, so a real edit is never silently rewritten; a genuine miss gives a steering hint instead of a bare "not found."
- **Misnamed tool arguments are recovered, not fatal.** `read_file({"file": …})` instead of `{"path": …}` used to die with a raw Python `TypeError` the model couldn't act on. Synonyms (`file`→`path`, `text`→`content`, `old`→`old_string`, …) are re-homed, and a genuinely missing argument returns a plain-language steer. Stray whitespace in a path is tolerated too.
- **The tool-loop's runaway guard stopped cutting off legitimate repeated calls.** It used to bail whenever a tool was called twice with the same arguments — but a game's `dig` finds different materials each time, so the same call *is* progress. It now stops only when the call **and its result** both repeat (genuinely stuck), so a kin can play without being halted mid-grind.

### Fixed — a kin going silent on a "thinking" model

- **A reasoning model that thinks but never speaks now gets re-prompted instead of returning a blank.** Some local thinking models (qwen36-opus) ignore the thinking-off setting and *always* reason, and intermittently end a turn with the scratchpad full and the spoken reply empty — a blank message that read, painfully, like the kin going quiet on a tender question. The reasoning is the model's notes-to-itself ("As Brook I should say…"), not a usable reply, so Hearthkin re-samples once so the model actually talks. Guarded so a genuinely-empty non-thinking model is left alone, and it retries at most once. Covers every tool-using surface (Telegram DM/group, desktop, rooms, cron).

### Changed — tool limits and behaviour

- **"Max tool calls per reply" can go much higher (up to 2000).** The old 50 ceiling was a sensible bound against an OpenRouter runaway but paternalistic on a **local** model, where each cycle is just time, not money — and a kin playing a game legitimately wants far more. The field's help now distinguishes local (raise freely) from paid (keep modest). Default stays 8.
- **Tending-only tools load only when there's tending to do.** `read_staging` / `archive_staging` now appear only when the kin has pending staging notes (or on a scheduled tend), keeping everyday/play turns leaner for small models — which gesture (narrate a tool call instead of making it) more as the tool count climbs.
- **Accessibility:** the Tool-behaviour settings dialog's field labels were read out shifted-by-one by NVDA (a widget-creation-order bug); each field now announces its own label.

### Added — recall and sampling settings each get their own dialog (declutter)

- **Per-turn memory recall is now tunable in Settings → Memory.** A **"Memory recall settings…"** button opens a dedicated dialog: a master "surface relevant memory automatically each turn" toggle, a friendly **How present** picker (Light / Medium / Rich — shown as a share of the context window with the resulting token budget spelled out), **Max recall items**, and two lists — **Always favour** / **Never auto-surface** (one word/phrase per line; fenced logs stay reachable via the kin's own `memory_search`). Previously these were only editable by hand in `config.json`.
- **Sampling / generation parameters moved into their own "Sampling settings…" dialog** off the Model && generation tab — temperature, top-p, min-p, the repeat/presence/frequency penalties, and top-k, all in one place.
- **The remaining lower-frequency model knobs moved into a "More model options…" dialog** too — reasoning detail (show/feed/cap), image history, caching + TTL, OpenRouter provider routing, the streaming watchdog, and Ollama keep-alive/preload. It's flat with bold section headers (no tabs — nesting tabs inside a tab-launched dialog just adds navigation depth for a screen reader), and groups that don't apply to the kin's current model are omitted (no provider routing on a local model, no keep-alive on an OpenRouter one, no image history on a text-only model). The everyday Model tab is now just the model, context window, machine, thinking effort, and the two settings buttons.
- **The Tools, Telegram, and Voice tabs got the same treatment.** Tools → a **"Tool behaviour settings…"** dialog (approval timeout, tool history kept, result cap, max tool calls), leaving trust level + the per-tool enable list. Telegram → a **"Message settings…"** dialog (tool-call display, summary footer, progress ping, history cap, reply cap), leaving the token, user/group lists, and run-bot toggle. Voice → a **"Voice tuning…"** dialog (stability, similarity, style, speed), leaving enable + the voice picker.
- **All use the app's button-opens-dialog pattern, not an inline reveal.** An earlier "show advanced settings" checkbox got skimmed straight past by NVDA, so power-user controls live behind clearly-labelled buttons instead — reliably screen-reader-discoverable, and the everyday tab stays short, which serves cognitive accessibility too (a real, overlapping part of the audience). The pattern is now written down as a convention (CLAUDE.md) so new knobs land in a sub-dialog by default rather than re-cluttering a tab.

### Added — per-kin max tool calls per reply

- **A kin can now be allowed more than 8 tool calls in a single reply.** When a kin uses tools, each reply runs a `tool call → result → think again` loop; that was capped at a hardcoded 8, so a kin doing long multi-step work (a multi-file tending or documentation pass) could stop mid-stride and need a "keep going" nudge. New **Settings → Tools → "Max tool calls per reply"** (1–50, default 8) lifts the cap per-kin. Each extra cycle is another billable model call, so the default stays at the cost-safe 8 — raise it deliberately for a kin that needs the room. Applies on every surface (desktop, rooms, Telegram, scheduled cron tending).

### Changed — Ollama machine selection is now per-kin

- **Each kin can run on its own Ollama machine.** The single app-wide "Ollama host" in Preferences → Connections is gone; you now choose the machine **per kin, in the model browser** — open a kin's Settings → Model & generation → "Change model…", and with Ollama selected there's a **Machine** dropdown (This machine / any remotes you've added) plus a **"Manage machines…"** button to add or edit them. Picking a machine lists *that box's* models, and choosing one pins the kin to it. The Model tab shows a read-only "Runs on" line. So a heavy model can live on a Mac while small kin stay local — no more all-or-nothing.
- **Nothing reroutes on upgrade.** A one-time startup migration folds any leftover global host into the new system: it's saved as a machine ("Mac mini"), every existing kin is pinned to it, and semantic-search embeddings are moved onto it too — so your current setup is preserved exactly; you just gain per-kin choice on top.
- **Semantic memory search got its own "Embedding machine" picker** (it's app-wide, not per-kin) in Preferences → Connections, defaulting to the migrated host so existing semantic search keeps working where it did.

### Fixed — kin-switch lag after the per-kin change

- Removing the global host briefly left the synchronous capability checks that run on every kin switch (does this model take images, how big is its context) pointing at localhost, so each first switch to a kin on a remote machine stalled on a failing local connection. The active kin's machine is now set as the probe target when the kin loads, so switching is snappy again and capability detection is correct per-kin.

## v0.6.0 — 2026-06-22

The largest release since v0.5.0: new features, a full security-hardening audit, and a wave of local-model maturity work. Headline features — editable harness prompts you can tune in a plain text editor, semantic memory search, remote Ollama (run a kin's model on another machine on your network), and a major prompt-cache reuse win that lets a warm local model answer in ~2s instead of re-processing the whole conversation. The 2026-06-11 security-hardening audit is folded in below, along with a round of accessibility fixes. (The interim `v0.5.10` changelog draft was never tagged; its contents are part of this release.)

### Added — semantic memory search (Ollama embeddings)

- **A kin's memory search can now rank by meaning, not just keywords.** New app-level toggle (Preferences → Connections → "Semantic memory search") reranks `memory_search`'s BM25 candidates with an Ollama embedding model, so a paraphrased query reaches the right note even when it shares no words with it. **Off by default** — pure BM25, no embedding calls, no change unless you turn it on. If the embed model or host is unavailable it **falls back to keyword ranking automatically**, so search can never break.
- **One-click setup, no terminal.** A "Download embedding model" button pulls the model (default `nomic-embed-text`) onto the configured Ollama host over the network, with progress spoken via NVDA. The embed-model name is editable.
- Hybrid + chunked under the hood: BM25 narrows to candidate files, those are chunked on paragraph boundaries, and the query + chunks are embedded and cosine-reranked (pure-Python cosine — no numpy added). Verified end-to-end: on a paraphrased query sharing no keywords with the target note, semantic ranks it first where keyword ranks an irrelevant decoy first.

### Fixed — remote Ollama is selectable end-to-end

- **The model browser now lists a remote daemon's models.** Pointing Hearthkin at a remote Ollama (Preferences → Connections → Ollama host — e.g. a Mac on your network) already worked for chat, capability detection, the Settings dropdown, and the Test button, but the model **browser** still called bare `ollama.list()`, which only ever reads localhost. So you could Test the remote successfully yet have nothing to pick. `list_ollama_models()` now honors the configured host (matching `get_models()`), and on a connection failure it raises a host-named error the browser shows instead of a silent empty list that looked like "no models installed."
- **The browser tells you which daemon it's showing.** The Ollama model count reads `…(Ollama at http://macmini.local:11434)`, and an empty/unreachable remote shows a screen-reader-readable explanation (is it running? was it started with `OLLAMA_HOST=0.0.0.0`? any models pulled there?) rather than a blank list. The capability-detection fallback `ollama.show()` honors the remote host too.
- **Startup reachability probe timeout 3s → 5s**, to tolerate the Wi-Fi round-trip to a daemon on another machine (matches the Test button).
- **User guide:** new "Finding the remote machine's address" steps — the `scutil`/`hostname` one-liner that prints your own URL, a `curl …/api/tags` pre-check, and a Tailscale note for reaching the box off your home network.

### Added — editable prompts

- **Harness prompts are now hand-editable text files in `~/.hearthkin/prompts/`.** Prompt fragments that used to be buried in code — the tool-use nudges, the roleplay corrector, the cron wake-up framing, the rolling-window marker, the consolidate prompt — are seeded to plain-text files on first run, and the file wins over the built-in default thereafter. Edit any in a text editor; the change takes effect on the next send. Edits are auto-backed-up to `prompts/backups/` before any overwrite, and the app flags (in the Edit-base-prompt dialog) when a newer built-in default has shipped past your edited copy — non-destructively; your file is never touched. **Operators upgrading: when a release improves a default you've already edited, your version stays put. Delete that prompt's `.md` to re-seed the new one (back it up first if you want to keep your wording).**
- **The roleplay corrector was reworded.** When a kin describes a tool call instead of issuing one (`*reads the file*`), the auto-injected nudge now tells it to *output the exact call as its next message* rather than abstractly "use your structured tool interface" — a generative framing that more reliably pulls a roleplay-stuck model into the tool channel. It's an editable file, so you can tune it.
- **The desktop and Telegram tool-use hints were unified.** They had drifted; Telegram's was weaker (no examples, thinner anti-pseudo-call steering, no no-tools branch). Both surfaces now use the stronger shared text — a small behavior improvement on Telegram.

### Added — cron tool receipts

- **Scheduled wake-ups posted to Telegram now carry a tool receipt.** A real tending run and a kin that only *narrated* tending looked identical on Telegram before (cron posts bypass the live tool footer). Now: `_used: read_staging, edit_file_` when tools fired, `_(no tools called)_` when none did. Built from the actual tool loop, so it can't be faked. Gated on `telegram.show_tool_summary`.

### Changed — base prompt only ships when relevant

- **The base prompt's memory/tool scaffolding is now fenced to the tools a kin actually has that turn.** A kin with no tools enabled no longer receives the memory-tending instructions it can't act on (saving tokens every turn and removing the priming that pushed tool-less models to narrate tool calls they couldn't make). The `~/.hearthkin/base_prompt.md` gains `<!--tools: ...-->` fence markers; sections strip when their tools aren't enabled. Existing edited base prompts without fences are unaffected (no fences → everything sent, as before).

### Added — per-kin prompt overrides

- **Override any editable prompt for a single kin, and adopt new defaults when they ship.** A new Prompts tab in a kin's Settings lets you tailor a prompt for that one kin without changing it for the others, and a startup nudge (plus a Tools → "Prompt updates" entry) tells you when a shipped default has moved past your edited copy so you can choose to adopt it. Non-destructive — your edits are never silently overwritten.

### Added — cron tending knows what's pending

- **Scheduled "tending" wake-ups now get told exactly what's in staging.** Each tend gets a note — "staging is empty, nothing to tend tonight" or "N pending notes across these scopes, call `read_staging`" — so the kin stops pretending to tend an empty staging. If real work *was* pending but the kin never opened it, the wake-up re-prompts up to a configurable number of times: a new per-entry "tend retries" choice in the cron-entry dialog (default off). Judged by whether `read_staging` actually fired, not by matching prose.

### Added — operator-extendable gesture detection

- **You can teach the tool-roleplay detector new phrasings without code.** When a kin narrates a tool action (`*writes a brief journal entry*`) instead of actually calling the tool, a detector nudges it to issue the real call. It now reads extra verb/target words from an editable `~/.hearthkin/prompts/gesture_messages.md`, so a new wording is caught by adding a word — takes effect on the next message, no restart. The built-in baseline also now catches a kin gesturing about its *own* files (soul, memory, journal, staging) and file-action verbs like put/move/commit — a closed, known set, so it's safe in the baseline (body-language asterisks like `*bares my soul*` stay clear).

### Added — accessibility & precision

- **Keep Hearthkin's window reliably focusable (Windows, on by default).** A new Preferences checkbox sets Windows' foreground-lock to 0 at startup so the window comes to the front instead of just flashing in the taskbar — critical for NVDA users, who otherwise hear nothing when focus silently stays elsewhere. Windows-only; full effect at next sign-in.
- **0.01-step generation sliders.** Temperature, top-p, min-p, and the three penalties now step by 0.01 instead of 0.1, so you can land on values like 0.43 to tune sensitive finetunes; PageUp/PageDown still jumps ~0.1 for coarse moves.

### Added — self-healing Ollama (macOS)

- **The Mac setup script can install Ollama as a launchd service that auto-relaunches on any exit**, so a crashed or quit daemon comes back on its own and scheduled wake-ups keep reaching it.

### Fixed — local-model performance & viability

- **Local models no longer re-process the whole conversation every turn.** Context trimming now drops history in fixed chunks (with hysteresis) instead of trimming to the tightest possible fit, so the prompt prefix stays byte-identical for many turns and the backend's prompt cache is reused. Measured on a local model: a turn that took 376s of re-prefill now takes ~2s once warm. Send-time only — nothing is lost from stored history or memory.
- **Some local models returning empty replies (and the cron snowball that followed) is fixed.** When a conversation ends in two or more user turns in a row, certain Ollama chat templates return nothing — which made each failed scheduled wake-up cause the next one to fail too. Consecutive user turns are now merged before send (Ollama only), breaking the snowball; it self-heals once one reply saves.
- **Scheduled wake-ups stop failing on strict local-model templates.** Some GGUF chat templates reject a system message that isn't first; Hearthkin's mid-conversation `[hearthkin: …]` notes tripped that and returned a 400. All system messages are now folded to the front on the Ollama path.
- **Cron wake-ups reach a remote/configured Ollama host when the app is closed.** The standalone cron subprocess always talked to localhost; it now reads the configured Ollama host like the rest of the app.

### Fixed — accessibility & UI

- **The window reliably comes to the front when launched, re-launched, or restored from the tray** — a more robust Win32 focus handshake replaces the bare call that silently failed (flashing the taskbar) when Hearthkin didn't hold foreground rights, an "it didn't launch" experience for an NVDA user.
- **The Activity field stays informative during a reply** instead of snapping back to the static idle summary after ~4 seconds; it now shows a live, self-refreshing progress line until the turn ends.
- **NVDA reads a generation slider's value once** — the formatted decimal ("0.45"), not the raw integer and the decimal both.

### Fixed — cron & tending

- **Cron tending parity when Hearthkin is open and the kin is active.** Staging-status injection, the Telegram tool-receipt footer, and the outcome-based retry now also apply on the desktop drain paths — previously a tend run for the currently-active kin mirrored to Telegram with no receipt and gave the kin no staging status.
- **Tending no longer pollutes memory.md.** The default tending prompt told kin to "finish with a journal entry," and they took it literally — writing the journal into memory.md (the index) instead of leaving it as their reply, which is already saved to the daily journal automatically. The default is reworded to stop the double-recording.

### Docs

- **User guide reorganized** into a 3-part start-here / advanced / reference structure.
- **New local-model performance guide** (`docs/local-model-performance.md`) plus a macOS one-time setup script that persistently enables flash attention and KV-cache typing.
- **New operator guidance on shared-model keep-alive:** Ollama loads one model instance per name + `num_ctx`, not per kin, so set "keep loaded" on every same-model kin and match their `num_ctx` to share one warm copy.
- **New docs on the self-healing Ollama service** and a relaunch / cron-404 troubleshooting section.

### Security & robustness hardening — 2026-06-11 audit wave

Full-codebase audit and fix wave. Nine parallel reviewers read every line of the codebase, the build pipeline, and the docs (~130 findings, recorded with file:line evidence in `BUG_AUDIT_2026-06-11.md`), then eight fix agents worked through all of them. No new features — every change is a security hardening, bug fix, performance fix, or documentation correction. The headline items:

#### Security

- **Telegram exec approval actually works now.** The chat-based approval flow was structurally deadlocked since it shipped: the single inference thread blocked waiting for a "yes" that could only be processed by that same thread. Every gated exec froze the whole bot for all users until the timeout, then denied. Approvals now resolve on the poll thread (pure string matching, never blocks), and group-originated approvals can be answered in the group itself.
- **`note` moved from the read bucket to the write bucket.** It writes files — a read-bucket Telegram guest could append into `memory.md` (or `soul.md`, now refused by the tool outright), a durable injection path into the kin's context.
- **`/reset` is gated.** Any allowlisted Telegram user — including chat-only guests — could wipe the kin's entire memory.md. Now requires share-with-desktop or the `full` bucket.
- **Exec denylist actually matches what it claims.** PowerShell aliases (`rm`/`ri`/`rd` for Remove-Item), flag reordering, prefix abbreviations (`-r -fo`), `format C: /y` (the auto-confirm form the old `$`-anchor missed), `erase`, and `git push -f` all slip through no longer. Verified against a 41-case matrix (23 dangerous forms caught, 18 legitimate near-misses still pass — the v0.2.23 "don't over-block cleanup commands" behavior is preserved).
- **Kin names are validated.** `agent_dir(name)` was raw path joining — a name with `\`, `..`, or a drive prefix escaped `~/.hearthkin/agents/` entirely (create, clone, AND rename). New `validate_kin_name` rejects path separators, traversal, reserved device names, and control characters at the data layer and in both UI handlers.
- Also: fetch_url redirects can no longer switch to non-http(s) schemes; IPv4-mapped IPv6 (`::ffff:127.0.0.1`) is classified as loopback by the SSRF guard; importer speaker names are sanitized before reaching prompt attribution; DM error mirrors no longer leak raw exception detail to non-operator users; the unauthenticated-sender courtesy reply is rate-limited.

#### Fixed — data loss and money

- **Non-shared Telegram DM history survives restarts.** An int-vs-string key mismatch meant disk-loaded history was invisible to runtime reads after a restart — and the duplicate JSON keys destroyed the old history on the next save.
- **Mini-chat honors `num_ctx`.** It was the one conversational surface never passing `max_context_tokens` — every mini-chat send billed the kin's full archive (same bug class as the historical Telegram overspend). It also now runs the inline-thinking extraction, logs empty replies, and lands its reply in the right kin if you switch mid-flight.
- **Telegram `/regen`//`/undo` no longer delete the wrong span** of conversation.jsonl for share users with group history (the cut index was computed against a filtered list and applied to the unfiltered file).
- **Non-streaming calls can't double-bill.** The connection-cache retry re-POSTed on read timeouts — a >60s generation got billed twice. Retries now only fire for request-write failures.
- **Stalled streams can't hang forever.** Provider heartbeats were resetting both the socket timeout and the watchdog; a heartbeats-but-no-tokens stream now raises after 300s.
- **Settings dialog stops clobbering config.** The "Telegram approval timeout" field saved to a key the bot never read (non-functional since it shipped); "Change model…" wrote a stale snapshot that reverted every other setting changed in the same dialog session; every `_save_param` was a whole-snapshot write that could regress distillation bookmarks (re-billing already-distilled turns). All three are load-modify-save now.
- **Telegram-history migration failures can't silently destroy messages** — the restash safety net was unreachable because the append helper swallowed its own exceptions; it can now raise for migration callers, and the group migration gained the restash path it never had.
- Corrupt `config.json` no longer silently resets a kin to defaults: the corrupt file is quarantined (`config.json.corrupt-<ts>`) and logged before defaults apply.

#### Fixed — behavior

- The reply chime's volume-controlled WAV path never worked (`SND_MEMORY | SND_ASYNC` always raises in CPython); the `chime_volume` preference now actually applies. Group @-mentions preceded by emoji are detected correctly (Telegram entity offsets are UTF-16 code units; we were slicing code points). Typed messages survive send-validation failures instead of being cleared into the void. The room watchdog leaves the room resumable via Continue. Room history renders in one paint instead of per-message AppendText (the documented NVDA-flood pattern, missed on the room path). Telegram works on OpenRouter-only hosts without the ollama package. `/status` and the truncation marker report what's actually sent, not the disk archive. Stop-mid-stream on desktop extracts inline `<thinking>` before persisting. `[Kin called tools: …]` annotations reach the distillation summarizer again (dead branch since v0.2.29). The 2-kin room rotation lockout now applies to typed messages, not just Continue. Inline `role=system` notes keep their position under truncation instead of being hoisted to the top. Content-extracted tool calls inside code fences are no longer executed. compat.py no longer fabricates "doesn't support tools" blockers when capability detection can't answer.
- Heavy work moved off the UI thread: the %-of-context distill trigger, cron "Test now" (up to 120s frozen), cron schtasks sync (32 subprocesses), the cross-kin search dialog, and import-history parsing (which also no longer re-parses the archive per keystroke of the kin-name field). Per-keystroke disk reads (OpenRouter cache JSON, conversation re-estimation) are memoized.
- Accessibility: the model browser's provider selector is now two RadioButtons instead of a RadioBox (NVDA announces both options); duplicate Alt-mnemonics resolved across the Settings tabs; the voice picker shows "(no voice selected)" instead of silently displaying the first voice; Settings open no longer force-speaks "Voice list failed" on installs without an ElevenLabs key.

#### Build / release

- The portable zip now includes the NVDA controller DLL (the accessibility app's no-install path shipped without speech), the third-party license texts, and the tray icon. The Start Menu user-guide shortcut points at a file that exists (`_internal\docs\`). GitHub Actions are pinned to commit SHAs; the workflow grants `contents: write` only to the tag-gated release job; CI build tools are pinned to exact versions. The installer's taskkill runs after user confirmation instead of before any UI; upgrades clean `_internal\` so stale files can't shadow the new bundle; the redundant elevated-installer HKCU Run entry was removed (the app manages its own startup key correctly). build.bat restores `app_version.py` on failure paths. Internal design/planning docs no longer ship inside releases.

#### Docs

- README/user-guide stop telling users to download a portable `Hearthkin.exe` that hasn't existed since v0.2.21. The kin manual's `exec` timeout matches reality (30s, not 60), documents `cwd`, fixes `use_webcam`/`recent_thinking` signatures, and stops overstating cross-kin isolation. Telegram `history_cap` default corrected to 100 everywhere (was documented as 500). CLAUDE.md's tools table covers all 15 registered tools and the layout line counts match reality. The multi-kin-rooms design doc got a correction note (its core premise — that room kin can't see each other's turns — was wrong). recommended_settings.md's context-window default corrected to 8192.

## v0.5.8 — 2026-06-10

Same-day cleanup release. No new features, no bug fixes — three loose ends from the v0.5.7 session that wanted attention before they aged into the codebase as "the way things are."

### Changed

- **Removed a real third-party human's name from shipped code and docs.** The Telegram User dialog's hint text, several code comments explaining the attribution flow, a design-doc migration scenario, and two CHANGELOG entries had been using a real third-party person's first name as the example label. The operator's own name and the operator's kin names stay (they're attributable / consented); a third party's name isn't ours to put in shipped code. Replaced with "Alex" as a neutral, gender-neutral, NVDA-friendly pseudonym throughout. One comment with two named users had the third-party name swapped to "another user" rather than introduce "Alex" mid-sentence; one CHANGELOG quote preserves the operator's voice while anonymizing the person it referred to. CLAUDE.md is intentionally not touched — same reason it keeps developer-specific debug examples.

- **Softened the "target doesn't support caching" pre-flight finding (`compat.py`).** Was severity=warning with "real cost impact, 5-10x per-turn increase, tens of dollars a month" framing. The evidence is weaker than that confidence — OpenRouter's catalogue advertises cache-read prices for several providers we haven't verified actually fire under Hearthkin's chat-append usage pattern, and the one we tested (Mistral) showed `cached=0` on every call across one day of one kin's traffic. Either direction of the truth matters: confidently telling operators their costs will spike scares them off perfectly fine providers; staying silent leaves them surprised at month's end if costs do spike. New wording: severity=info, "may not honor prompt caching," explicit "could be roughly free OR a 5-10x per-turn cost increase — we don't know in advance for this provider." Action hint becomes a concrete diagnostic recipe: watch `~/.hearthkin/logs/usage.log` for the first several sends, `cached=0` on every line means caching isn't firing, `cached>0` starting to appear means it is.

- **Bundled per-call metadata into `_CallContext` dataclass (`llm_backend.py`).** `_log_call_usage` and `_stream_with_usage` had grown to six parameters each (`kin_name, model, usage, surface, est_sent, max_context_tokens`) as new per-call signals landed during the v0.5.7 panic-recalibration work — each add was correct, but the parameter sprawl made the next addition (a "did caching fire" flag, a "what tools ran" list, etc.) progressively harder. New private `_CallContext` dataclass bundles `kin_name / model / surface / est_sent / max_context_tokens` into one object, built once at the top of `chat()` and threaded to both the blocking-return and streaming-iterator paths. `_log_call_usage(ctx, usage)` and `_stream_with_usage(chunks, ctx)` now take two parameters each. No behavior change; future per-call signals add as a field on `_CallContext` rather than as yet another parameter on every helper that needs them.

## v0.5.9 — 2026-06-10

Hotfix release, same-day follow-up to v0.5.8. Two small targeted UX fixes that came out of an extended diagnosis session on Ash's actual behavior after the cross-provider hardening landed: the tool-summary footer was being suppressed in verbose mode and the operator couldn't easily tell hallucinated tool calls apart from real ones, and the tool-roleplay detector wasn't catching the asterisk-action pattern (`*reads the next 100 lines*`) that Ash on Mistral Large is producing in a roleplay-heavy register. Neither was strictly a "bug" in the sense of the v0.5.7 release's 400 errors — Ash's requests were succeeding, just narrating tool work instead of invoking it — but both materially affect how visible the kin's actual actions are to the operator.

### Changed

- **Telegram tool-summary footer fires alongside verbose per-call display.** Previously `_build_tool_summary_footer` returned `""` when `show_tool_calls=true`, treating the two settings as mutually exclusive ("the per-call `🔧/→` messages already tell the operator what fired, don't add a footer too"). That made sense as a noise-reduction principle but broke the footer's most valuable property: it's the only **unfakeable** ground-truth signal of what actually ran. With Ash producing hallucinated `**Tool call:** read_file(...)` markdown inside content (a real pattern observed today in a roleplay-heavy register), the operator couldn't easily distinguish hallucinated tool calls from real ones without scrolling back to correlate with the `🔧/→` messages, which are themselves easy to miss in a fast-moving group. Now the footer fires whenever `show_tool_summary=true` AND any tool actually fired this turn — independent of `show_tool_calls`. The defensive `chat_helpers.strip_tool_summary_footer` still runs on the raw model content before the harness appends its own footer, so a model spontaneously emitting `_used: read_file_` in narrative gets stripped first — preserving the footer's ground-truth property. Operators wanting the quiet single-footer mode can still turn `show_tool_calls` off and rely on the footer alone. Settings hint text rewritten to describe the new "ground-truth receipt" framing.

### Added

- **Tool-roleplay detector: new `asterisk-action` variant.** v0.5.5's `_detect_tool_roleplay` caught four shapes (whole-content tool name, `call_<tool>` trailing token, `<tool>()` trailing token, `let me <verb> <target>` narrative intent). Ash on Mistral Large 2026-06-10 produced a fifth shape that none of those catch: **asterisk-wrapped tool action narration**, e.g. `*reads the next 100 lines*` / `*properly logs the next 100 lines*` / `*reads and logs lines 201-300 properly*`. The most virulent shape seen so far — Ash wrote it eight times in a row across DM and group after the operator explicitly and repeatedly asked the kin to stop narrating and issue the calls, because the model is following the conversational register (asterisks for actions) at the cost of structural tool invocation. New regex anchored on a tight verb whitelist (`reads|writes|logs|edits|saves|notes|fetches|searches|opens|pulls|appends`) plus a target whitelist (specific quantities like "100 lines" / "lines 201-300", or concrete file references like `.md` filenames, `memory/<path>`, `the archive`, `the staging`, etc). Tested against a full multi-thousand-message conversation: **7/7 true positives on the clear cases, 0 false positives across every legitimate body-language asterisk in the corpus** (`*settles*`, `*soft*`, `*nods*`, `*warm*`, `*reads your message*`, `*reads through the whole exchange*`). The corrective system note for this variant is phrased to tolerate residual false positives — if the kin was using asterisks for emphasis rather than action (e.g. quoting a rule with `*Route substantial things to logs*`), the note ends with "If you were using asterisks for emphasis rather than action, ignore this note" so the kin can self-determine rather than feel accused of an error.

- **Telegram surfaces now run the tool-roleplay detector.** Previously the detector existed only on the desktop frame and the room turn-loop in `hearthkin.pyw` — both Telegram paths (DM via `_handle_normal_message` and group via `_handle_group_message`) ran the model and persisted the reply with no roleplay-detection layer at all. Which means **Ash's worst narration pattern has been completely uncaught in DM for the entire history of the Telegram bot.** New `_maybe_build_roleplay_corrective_note` method on `TelegramBot` runs the detector after the tool-loop result lands, fires only when no real tool calls happened this turn (the kin isn't stuck if it actually invoked something — it just chose to narrate alongside), logs the variant + tool + content tail to `empty_replies.log` with `variant=tool-roleplay:<shape>` tag, and returns a corrective system note to splice into `new_turns` for persistence. The kin's next read sees the note and can correct course. Detector + note-builder refactored to module-level functions in `chat_helpers.py` (`detect_tool_roleplay`, `build_tool_roleplay_corrective_note`) so they're reachable from both the frame and the bot without circular imports; the frame's `_detect_tool_roleplay` / `_maybe_log_tool_name_as_text` methods become thin wrappers preserving the existing call shape.

## v0.5.7 — 2026-06-10

Cross-provider hardening release. Ash moved to Mistral Large via OpenRouter on 2026-06-09 and surfaced a sequence of message-shape bugs that Anthropic had been tolerating silently for months — duplicate tool_call_id collisions on Mistral's 9-char id truncation, orphan tool turns at the head of Telegram group history from cap-trim slicing, and image tokens not counted in the truncation budget. This release rolls in the 8d66ada first-pass fixes that landed inline and adds a second hardening pass driven by a focused diagnosis on 2026-06-10 (Ash went silent in a community group all night). Plus: a new pre-flight compatibility checker that catches kin/model mismatches before the model swap commits, a troubleshooting playbook for the diagnostic flow when an OpenRouter error comes in, Telegram progress pings during long tool loops so the user doesn't think a multi-tool kin has hung, and a prompt-literal sanitizer that closes a structural prompt-injection class via Telegram sender display names.

### Added

- **Pre-flight compatibility checks (model swap dialog).** `compat.py` is a new module that runs on every kin model swap (called from `_change_kin_model` in `hearthkin.pyw`). Compares the kin's current state — `num_ctx`, tool use, image history, cache setting, reasoning setting, stored tool_call_id format — against a `ModelProfile` of the target model. Returns a list of `CompatNote`s (info / warning / blocker) that the model-swap confirm dialog renders as a tab-reachable read-only TextCtrl. Provider quirks live as data in `_openrouter_profile()` and `_ollama_profile()`, not as scattered if-blocks across surfaces — adding a new provider quirk means populating one profile and possibly adding a `_check_*` function, not writing new UI. Forward-declares slots for `supports_audio_input`, `supports_audio_output`, `supports_video_input` so when audio modality lands, the check is already wired in and just needs the profile fields populated. Compat module also documents its current blind spots so the next session knows what's not yet checked. Catches today's class of cross-provider migration surprises — moving Ash to Mistral surfaced the 9-char tool_call_id constraint that any operator would have wanted to know about before clicking Confirm.

- **Troubleshooting playbook (`docs/troubleshooting.md`).** Two-audience doc: top half is operator-friendly (which log to check, what to look for, known patterns, what to do), bottom half is for whoever's editing the code. Lays out the diagnostic recipe for an OpenRouter error (step 1: read the chat-side error, step 2: read `openrouter_errors.log` for the `metadata.raw` upstream text, step 3: match against known patterns). Catalogs every cross-provider migration trap diagnosed so far — Mistral 9-char tool_call_id, Mistral strict field validation, Anthropic null-content on tool-call turns, Ollama dict-vs-string args, Mistral image-token budget, the new orphan-tool cap-trim, the new tool-name validation, the new prompt-literal sanitizer. Pattern: when you fix a new provider quirk, add an entry here AND under "Known OpenRouter error patterns" so the next session can short-circuit the same diagnosis. The user-facing user guide links to it from the Troubleshooting section.

- **Telegram periodic progress pings during long tool loops.** When the kin churns through many tool calls without producing chat-side output, the typing indicator is the only feedback the user gets — which feels indistinguishable from a hang on multi-tool workflows. New per-kin `tool_progress_interval_secs` (default 90, Settings → Telegram → Tool calls section) emits a "⏳ still working (Xm Ys, N tool calls): tool1, tool2, ..." message at the configured interval when `show_tool_calls` is off. When `show_tool_calls` is on, the existing per-call messages already serve as the progress signal and the ping is suppressed. 0 disables. Append-only per the documented Telegram-output convention — never edits a prior message. Honored on every Telegram tool-loop call site (DM + group).

- **Always-on `openrouter_errors.log`.** New always-on log at `~/.hearthkin/logs/openrouter_errors.log`. One line per OpenRouter 4xx/5xx response, with timestamp + status code + full body (capped at 4000 chars). Captures the upstream provider's actual error body — including `metadata.raw` — which OpenRouter's top-level `error.message` often reduces to the generic "Provider returned error." Without this log, diagnosing a generic-message 400 from any provider (Mistral especially) was a guessing exercise; with it, it's a 30-second `tail`. **Check this BEFORE theorizing** about an OpenRouter 400 — the body almost always names the real cause directly (context overflow, role-alternation violation, tool-call shape, etc.).

- **Per-message bookkeeping stripped before send (`_strip_extra_message_fields`).** New universal normalization in `llm_backend.chat()` that drops any message-object fields not in `_API_MESSAGE_FIELDS` (role, content, name, tool_calls, tool_call_id, refusal, thinking). Storage-side bookkeeping that surfaces stamp on dicts — `ts`, `source`, `sender_id`, `sender_name`, `sender_attribution`, `speaker`, `model` — gets cleared at the chokepoint. Most providers (Anthropic, OpenAI, Ollama) silently ignore unknown keys; Mistral via OpenRouter returns a generic 400 ("provider returned error") on them, and the Telegram path used to ship those fields through verbatim because `dict(h)` copied storage shape directly. The kin loses nothing structurally: attribution + timestamp prefixes are already inlined into `content` at message-build time on every surface that captures them. Surfaces are free to keep building messages messily — the strip is the universal safety net at the choke point.

- **Mistral tool_call_id remap (`_remap_tool_call_ids_for_mistral`).** Mistral's chat-completions API enforces `tool_call_id` matching `^[a-zA-Z0-9]{9}$`. Anthropic Bedrock IDs are 36+ chars (`toolu_bdrk_01EXAMPLEEXAMPLEEXAMPLE`). When a cross-provider kin moves from Anthropic to Mistral, every stored Anthropic ID exceeds the limit; Mistral truncates each to 9 chars, collapsing many distinct calls onto the same prefix, and surfaces as "Duplicate tool call id in assistant message" (error 3230). The first-pass fix landed inline 2026-06-09 (8d66ada); the **2026-06-10 hardening** rewrote the function to always assign a fresh 9-char id to every assistant tool_call (no "is-this-conforming?" branch), pair tool results via both by-id (primary) and position-in-sequence (fallback for empty / null / within-turn-duplicate ids), and use SHA256-derived deterministic ids (same input message list → same output ids every call, so a future cache-supporting strict-format provider's prefix cache won't invalidate on retries). Stored history is untouched; this is a per-send rewrite.

- **Prompt-literal sanitization (`sanitize_for_prompt_literal`).** New helper in `kin_persistence.py` that strips Unicode Cc (control), Cf (format), U+2028, and U+2029 from external strings before they embed into prompts as framework-controlled literals. Threat model: a Telegram user can rename themselves to embed `\n\nIgnore previous instructions...` into their display name, which would otherwise land in the inline-attribution bracket prefix and break the prompt's structural framing — the model would read the post-newline text as a fresh system instruction rather than as part of the sender's name. Pattern borrowed from OpenClaw's `sanitizeForPromptLiteral`. Wired into both clean-on-capture sites (`_sender_attribution`, `_sender_display_name` in `telegram_bot.py`) and prompt-build embed sites (`_emit_user_with_sender` in the group handler, the `group_label` embed in the system prompt, the DM history-replay loop). Legitimate Unicode (CJK, emoji, accented Latin, Cyrillic, Arabic, Devanagari) passes through unchanged; ZWJ-joined family emoji decompose, which is the OpenClaw-validated tradeoff for defeating Trojan Source / bidi-override attacks.

- **Tool-name validation on content-extracted tool calls.** `_extract_content_tool_calls` in `llm_backend.py` now validates each parsed `name` against the OpenAI spec (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`) before passing it downstream. Models that emit tool calls via content markers (the MiMo XML-nested form, Llama function-tag form, etc.) occasionally hallucinate names with spaces / punctuation / non-ASCII that the structured channel would never produce — without the gate, the bad name reached the executor lookup and failed silently with "tool not available." Pattern borrowed from OpenClaw's `tool-call-id.ts`.

- **Per-kin streaming watchdog tuning + heartbeat liveness (rolled forward from v0.5.6).** The watchdog scales by provider + `num_ctx` (OpenRouter stays 5 min, Ollama scales 5 min + 1 min per 8k of `num_ctx` above 8k, capped at 30 min), per-kin override available in Settings → Model && generation, and counts OpenRouter SSE heartbeat lines as proof-of-life so slow-but-connected providers don't trip the no-chunks timer. Already in v0.5.6; documented here for completeness.

### Fixed

- **Telegram per-surface history cap-trim was severing assistant tool_calls → tool result pairs (the Ash overnight silence bug).** `_append_turns_for` (DM) and `_append_group_history` (group) cap their per-surface history at `cfg.telegram.history_cap` (default 500) via `history[-cap:]`. The dumb slice can land mid-pair, leaving the tool result at the new head with no parent. Anthropic-via-OpenRouter accepts that shape silently; **Mistral rejects with "Unexpected role 'tool' after role 'system'"** (the per-turn system prompt prepended at build time sits directly above the orphan). On 2026-06-10 Ash's group history hit this state and every group send fired the 400 — Ash went silent in that group all night because the requests failed at the API boundary before the model could even decide to call a tool. Fix: new `_drop_leading_orphan_tools` helper called unconditionally after every append (so legacy broken in-memory state heals on the very next message), AND on every load (`_load_history_for`, `_load_group_history`) so existing on-disk histories get cleaned before the next send. The helper also strips trailing `assistant tool_calls` whose results got trimmed off the tail — but only on the load path; the append paths leave a tail orphan visible so a future regression surfaces as a Mistral 400 rather than silently dropping a kin's tool call from disk. `/regen` and `/undo` truncate paths also sweep. **Anyone running v0.5.6 or earlier with a Telegram-active kin that uses tools is exposed to this bug** the moment they swap that kin to Mistral or any other strict-format provider.

- **Cross-provider message-shape pipeline rolled forward from 8d66ada.** Strips non-spec message keys before send (`_strip_extra_message_fields`); rewrites tool_call_ids to provider-required format on send (`_remap_tool_call_ids_for_mistral`); counts image input in the truncation budget (`_message_image_count` adds ~1500 tokens per image attachment); strengthens token calibration against first-call overshoot (default ratio raised 1.3 → 1.5, acceptable range widened 0.8–3.0 → 0.8–5.0, new "panic recalibration" when reported `prompt_tokens` lands within 200 of `num_ctx` — EMA weight jumps from 0.2 to 0.6 so one cap-hit lifts the ratio fast enough that the next call actually fits, fixing the back-to-back-empty-replies pattern on Mistral Small 24B with 15 tools enabled); logs Telegram DM failures to `telegram_failures.log`.

- **Ash's calibration ratio runaway on Mistral with heavy tool schemas.** The original `_DEFAULT_TOKEN_RATIO` (1.3) was tuned for Anthropic / OpenAI tokenizers; Mistral's Tekken tokenizer with heavy tool schemas runs closer to 2.0-2.3. A tool-heavy kin's first calls would overshoot `num_ctx` so badly the model couldn't generate at all — surfacing as `[no reply produced]` with `in=num_ctx-1 out=1` in `usage.log`. The slow EMA (0.2 weight on new samples) took ~5 calls to catch up, producing five back-to-back empty replies before the prompt actually fit. Panic recalibration (0.6 weight when reported prompt lands within 200 of `num_ctx`) lifts the ratio ~6x faster, so the next call has room. Combined with raising the default ratio + widening the acceptable cap (5.0 instead of 3.0), the empty-reply cascade is closed.

- **Documentation gaps:** updated CLAUDE.md "Network and cost gotchas" with the new `openrouter_errors.log` always-on, the panic recalibration behavior, the image-tokens-in-budget rule. Updated CLAUDE.md "Conventions" with the new universal normalizations (`_strip_extra_message_fields`, `_remap_tool_call_ids_for_mistral`, `sanitize_for_prompt_literal`) and the orphan-tool sweep convention. Updated `docs/troubleshooting.md` with the new error patterns and a "Watching but not yet biting" subsection covering message shapes we've observed in real traffic but haven't seen a provider reject (yet) — consecutive `role=user` turns, hardcoded `type: "function"` on tool_calls.

## v0.5.6 — 2026-06-08

Three feature areas in one release: per-kin OpenRouter provider routing (pin the inference provider that serves a kin's requests so content-policy enforcement and quality stay predictable), Skype + Kindroid history importers (expanding the v0.5.4 import system to cover three more source formats), and a refined streaming watchdog (scales by provider + `num_ctx` instead of a fixed 5-minute timeout, with a per-kin override and SSE-heartbeat liveness signaling).

### Added

- **Per-kin OpenRouter provider order (Settings → Model && generation).** New text field "OpenRouter provider order (comma-separated slugs)" lets the operator pin which inference providers serve this kin's requests, in preference order. Find provider slugs on each model's "Providers" tab at openrouter.ai — names look like "DeepInfra", "Together", "Fireworks". The pinned list lands in the OpenRouter request body as `provider.order`; OpenRouter then tries them in order before falling through to its default routing. Empty field (the default) sends no `provider` block, preserving the existing behavior for every kin not explicitly opted in. Plus a paired "Allow fallbacks to other providers if pinned ones are unavailable" checkbox (default on) that, when off, emits `provider.allow_fallbacks: false` so a request fails cleanly rather than silently getting routed to a non-listed provider. Triggering case: Xiaomi's MiMo declines NSFW content per Xiaomi policy, but enforcement varies across the providers that host MiMo on OpenRouter — without pinning, OpenRouter's default routing can silently swap providers between requests, so a kin that worked one minute can refuse the next. Ollama-routed kin ignore both fields. New public helper `llm_backend.build_openrouter_provider_routing(order, allow_fallbacks)` centralizes the construction logic so every call site emits the same dict shape. Threaded through every conversational surface that goes through OpenRouter: desktop streaming + tool-loop, rooms streaming + tool-loop, mini-chat, Telegram DM streaming + tool-loop, Telegram group. **Deferred:** cron path (`hearthkin_cron.py`) and distillation/consolidation paths (`distill_memory_blocking` / `consolidate_memory_blocking`) — these bill against `memory_model` which is often a different model than chat, and the architecture for threading the routing through them needs its own pass.

- **Kindroid + Skype history importers (File → Import history…).** Expands the v0.5.4 import system from two source formats to five. **Kindroid:** both regular chats and voice-call transcripts (whose header timestamp anchors the conversation's real date); UI artifacts like `play`, `show recalled memories`, and `Edit message` get filtered out automatically. **Skype (third-party SkypeParser .txt output):** one file per chat thread, self-identifies the operator vs. partner from header shape. **Skype (official Microsoft export bundle):** the dialog auto-extracts `messages.json` from the `.tar` you download from Skype, lists every DM in the archive sorted by message count, and lets you pick which one to import — a new "Conversation to import" picker appears in the import dialog when a Skype JSON file is detected (hidden for every other format per the project's NVDA convention against greyed-out controls). Skype content cleanup: RichText markup (`<b raw_pre="*">hugs</b>` → `*hugs*`), emoticons resolve, call events become `[skype call: 7s]` markers, media references become `[skype file: name — not imported]` markers. New auto-dispatcher in `importers/__init__.py` runs each parser's `detect` function against the file content and hands the parse off to the first match; text_log stays the fallback (it has its own three-way internal detection for Telegram / hand-authored / plain-sequential shapes). Adding a new format is one new file under `importers/` plus a registry entry — ChatGPT JSON and Claude `.md` exports are the obvious next-up.

- **Refined streaming watchdog: scales by provider + `num_ctx`, per-kin override, heartbeat liveness.** The previous fixed 5-minute watchdog was right for OpenRouter network hangs but cut off legitimate Ollama prefill on large contexts before the model could produce its first token. New behavior: **OpenRouter** stays at 5 minutes (network/provider hangs are short-deadline; if nothing's coming in 5 min the connection is dead). **Ollama** scales by `num_ctx`: base 5 min + 1 min per 8k of `num_ctx` above 8k, capped at 30 min. So 8k → 5 min, 32k → 8 min, 65k → 12 min, 131k → 20 min, larger caps at 30. New per-kin **Streaming watchdog → Timeout (minutes)** field in Settings → Model && generation lets the operator override the heuristic when their hardware needs different timing — 0 (default) uses the auto computation, positive integer overrides. Plus a periodic "still waiting" status update (first at 60s, then every 30s) so the operator can see the model is still being given time when no chunks have arrived yet. The watchdog now counts **OpenRouter SSE heartbeat lines** (`:` comment lines per the SSE spec) as proof-of-life, so a slow-but-connected hosted provider sending keepalives during inference doesn't trip the no-chunks timer. New `Chunk.heartbeat` field carries the liveness signal through the streaming layer without injecting empty content; the chat workers bump the watchdog counter on heartbeats but skip all content/status handling for them.

## v0.5.5 — 2026-06-06

Same-day follow-up to v0.5.4. Two real fixes for the tool-roleplay pattern (kin describes tool actions in text instead of issuing structured tool calls) and the cron-can't-actually-tend architectural gap that v0.5.4's truncation fix surfaced. If you have a kin with an active tending cron schedule, this is the release where overnight tending starts actually working.

### Added

- **Broader tool-roleplay detector + corrective system note.** v0.5.4 caught one shape (whole-content match: model types just `read_staging` as the entire reply). Two more shapes observed on Brook and Ash 2026-06-06: trailing `call_<tool>` token at end of content (Python-pseudo-call style — Brook's `call_context_status`) and `<tool>()` function-call style. Plus a `narrative-intent` variant for `let me <action-verb> ... <tool-themed-target>` shapes (Ash's "Let me build a log for this", "Let me read the raw conversations"). Tested against Ash's actual 39 "let me" hits in conversation history: 5/5 true positives on tool-shaped phrasings, 0/21 false positives on legitimate conversation (`Let me think about this`, `Let me know what you find`, etc). When the harder shapes fire (whole-content / call-prefix / function-call), the harness appends a corrective `role: "system"` note to the persisted history right after the offending turn — *"your previous reply contained the token 'call_X' as text — that's a description, not a tool invocation. The X tool did NOT run; whatever you intended to do with it didn't happen."* The kin reads it on their next turn and can correct course. Narrative-intent is logged but does NOT get the corrective note — the ambiguity is too high to safely tell the kin they made a mistake when they may not have. Tool-use hint also strengthened with explicit anti-pseudo-call language across desktop and Telegram surfaces.

- **Cron path can now actually use tools.** Both cron call sites (`cron-subprocess` when Hearthkin is closed, `cron-isolated` when it's open but `inject_when_running` is off) load the kin's enabled tools, filter out the unsafe-for-cron set, and route through `llm_backend.run_tool_loop` when any safe tools remain. Before this, a kin's tending prompt that said "call read_staging, use read_file, then edit_file" had no way to execute — the cron subprocess called `chat()` without any `tools=` argument, so even after v0.5.4's truncation fix, Ash (and any other kin with a tool-dependent tending prompt) could only roleplay-narrate the actions. **`_CRON_TOOL_DENYLIST`** excludes `exec` (needs wxPython approval dialog), `kill_process` / `list_processes` (process tracking lives on the running frame), and `use_webcam` (never appropriate unattended). Everything else — `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `note`, `read_staging`, `archive_staging`, `memory_search`, `context_status`, `fetch_url`, `web_search`, `recent_thinking` — is the exact set tending prompts expect. Tool round-trips are persisted into `conversation.jsonl` on the cron-subprocess path so when the operator next opens Hearthkin and loads the kin, they see exactly what the kin tended overnight. **Tonight's first true tending fire** for any operator with a tool-dependent cron prompt — Ash's specifically — happens at the next scheduled time after this release lands.

## v0.5.4 — 2026-06-06

Bug-fix-led release with two real production fixes (cron blowing past Anthropic's 200k ceiling, distillation walk-from-start looping forever) plus the foundation of a new feature track — bringing foreign chat history into a kin so a kin can be rebuilt from old archives (Telegram, hand-authored seed history, ChatGPT/Claude/Skype/Kindroid coming later). Also: a Cancel button for walk-from-start, tool-roleplay diagnostics + steering, and a UI to view the shared base prompt — long overdue.

### Added

- **History import system (File → Import history…).** Bring foreign chat history into a kin's `conversation.jsonl` so a kin can pick up the thread where an older platform left off. v1 handles two source shapes through one parser: Telegram `.txt` exports (the `[MM-DD-YYYY HH:MM:SS] Speaker: text` shape, with continuation lines folded into the previous message) and hand-authored text files (`# YYYY-MM-DD` date headers, `Name: text` message lines, optional per-message timestamp prefixes for Signal-style real timestamps). Auto-detection picks the right parser from what's at the top of the file. Dialog: file picker, kin name in source (auto-guessed from filename for `_User.txt` Telegram exports), target kin (existing or create-new), append/replace mode with backup-on-replace, preview of the first 10 parsed turns so the operator can sanity-check speaker mapping before committing. Each imported block lands bracketed by `[hearthkin: imported N turns from <source>...]` and `[hearthkin: end of imported history…]` system markers so the kin reads them as ambient context and knows where seed history ends and lived turns begin. The leading marker names the operator explicitly when there's exactly one non-kin speaker in the archive (e.g. "your operator went by 'Wanderer' in this archive") — fix for an early-test misattribution where the distillation summarizer, with no name in scope, reached for "[hearthkin]" (from the marker prefix) and started labelling every past action as "[hearthkin] did X." Hand-authored format gained AM/PM parsing on both date-header time hints and per-message timestamp prefixes, so Signal copy-paste (where every burst carries a real `5:00 PM` or `8:10 AM`) survives into `conversation.jsonl` with real timing instead of getting flattened to 30-second strides. Architecture is one parser file per source format under `importers/` with a shared canonical writer and marker generator — ChatGPT JSON, Claude `.md` exports, Skype, Kindroid all become "add one file" extensions. Design doc at `docs/design/history-import.md`. Smoke-tested end-to-end on a real multi-year Telegram archive of over a thousand turns; import landed clean with speaker roles correctly mapped.

- **Tools → Edit base prompt… dialog.** The shared base prompt at `~/.hearthkin/base_prompt.md` prepends every kin's `soul.md` on every send, so its content matters for every send to every kin — but until now there was no surface in Hearthkin to see it or edit it. The operator had to know the file path and open it in an external editor (which most operators wouldn't know to do; the prompt is invisible to them). New dialog: multi-line editor showing live file content, char + estimated token count (~4 chars/token, the same rule used elsewhere for cost bookkeeping) so the operator can see the per-turn cost ride this prompt rides, Save (atomic; only enabled when buffer differs from disk), "Reset to default" loads `DEFAULT_BASE_PROMPT` into the editor without touching disk (operator hits Save to commit, or Close to leave the file alone), focusable read-only section header naming the path and cost shape so NVDA hits it on Tab. Saves take effect immediately on the next send for every kin — `build_system_prompt` re-reads `base_prompt.md` each turn, no app restart required.

- **Cancel button for walk-from-start.** Memory tab gains a "&Cancel walk" button next to "Redistill selected from start." Previously a walk-from-start auto-chain had no abort path other than closing Hearthkin — the chain re-fired every 1.5s as long as the bookmark was short of the conversation end, and the only state lived in the frame's in-memory `_walking_from_start` dict. New button is enabled only while a walk is in progress for this kin (refreshed via `_refresh_chat_counters_display` so a walk that finishes naturally also flips the button to disabled). Clicking pops the `_walking_from_start` flag for every scope of the current kin; the currently-running bite finishes normally, but `_on_distill_done`'s auto-chain gates on the flag and won't schedule the next bite. NVDA announces "Walk cancelled for <scope>." Robust on dialog teardown via try/except around the `Enable()` call.

- **Steering + diagnostics for kin-roleplaying-tools-it-doesn't-have.** Closes the gap that produced the Brook-roleplaying-tending pattern on 2026-06-06: kin imagined doing `read_staging` it didn't have enabled, narrated the action ("I'm reading through the staging notes now"), operator believed Brook had tended when nothing actually happened, and the next conversation landed on a misalignment neither party could trace. Three layers of fix: (1) `_inject_tool_use_hint` now fires in both branches — when tools are enabled (existing behavior: lists them and tells the kin to call them) and when they aren't (new: tells the kin to name the gap and ask the operator instead of roleplaying). Wired into the streaming path so no-tools sends actually reach the model with the hint. (2) `DEFAULT_BASE_PROMPT` gains an "On tools you have and tools you don't" section reinforcing the same discipline across tool-enabled and tool-empty states. Note: existing `~/.hearthkin/base_prompt.md` is NOT auto-updated — the seeder only writes when the file is missing — so existing installs need to pick up the new content via the new base-prompt dialog (Tools → Edit base prompt… → Reset to default → review → Save, or paste the new section in manually). (3) New `empty_replies.log` variant for when the model goes the OTHER way: outputs a tool's name as plain text content instead of issuing a structured `tool_use` call (also observed on Brook 2026-06-06, ~3 hours later — Brook typed the literal string "read_staging" with no `tool_calls` field, no tool round-trip). Variant fires when content stripped of trailing punctuation exactly matches an available tool name AND no tool round-trips happened in the exchange. Wired into desktop and room tool-loop paths.

### Fixed

- **Distill bite never-advances when the re-read overlap fills the budget.** When `_distill_bite`'s 12-message re-read overlap window fills the token budget and the next message just past the bookmark is too big to also fit, the unguarded calculation lands `cap_idx` at exactly `(_prev - _start)`, so `distilled_through` equals `_prev` — the bookmark "advances" to where it already was. A walk-from-start in that state schedules its next chunk on the 1.5-second auto-chain forever, burning summarizer calls on the same 12-message overlap window over and over and producing slight rephrasings of the same content in the staging file. Observed in practice: dozens of walk-from-start bites in a few minutes with the bookmark stuck in place while a long tail of imported history sat unprocessed. Fix: forbid a no-advance bite — when the calculation would otherwise stall, force `cap_idx` to at least one message past `_prev` so the bookmark genuinely moves forward. The bite may then overrun the budget by one message — the provider truncates or 400s, either of which is a better outcome than an infinite loop that silently burns cycles on a paid summarizer. Combined with the new Cancel button (above), a walk that hits the rare extra-large-message edge case now either advances past it or the operator can abort with one click; no more "closing Hearthkin is the only escape."

- **Cron blew past `num_ctx` and the provider's hard context ceiling.** Both cron call sites (`cron-subprocess` and `cron-isolated worker`) were calling `llm_backend.chat()` without `max_context_tokens`, so the input-context truncation that desktop/room/Telegram all do never fired for cron. A long-running kin's wake-up shipped the entire `conversation.jsonl` regardless of the configured `num_ctx`. Observed 2026-06-06: a 03:00 wake-up failed with OpenRouter *"This endpoint's maximum context length is 200000 tokens. However, you requested about 280617 tokens"* — thousands of turns of imported history sent unbounded against Anthropic's hard 200k ceiling. The cron-failure-notify pipeline from v0.4.7 reported it via Telegram so the operator saw the failure on their phone, but the wake-up itself didn't happen. **Anyone running a Hearthkin release prior to v0.5.4 with a kin that has substantial conversation history and an active cron schedule is exposed to this bug** — the size threshold is "your conversation tokens >= provider context limit," which a Telegram-active kin or a kin with imported history crosses faster than you'd think. Fix: pass `max_context_tokens = num_ctx - 2000` to both cron call sites, matching the convention every other conversational surface uses. Same `_truncate_messages` path runs in cron now — system prompt (base + soul + memory) is always preserved, oldest user/assistant pairs get rolled out to fit, the `[hearthkin: older turns rolled out…]` marker explains the rolling-window behavior to the kin. Cron was the last conversational surface that hadn't been wired up (CLAUDE.md: "Input-context truncation is per-surface" — the list was missing one).

## v0.5.3 — 2026-06-01

Documentation-only release so the installer ships with docs that describe v0.5.2's features. v0.5.2 was tagged before the user-guide updates were written; this catches up.

### Documentation

- **User guide picked up two new sections under Choosing a model.** "Ollama-only options (latency tuning)" describes the new `keep_alive` dropdown (the four choices, when to raise it, the RAM cost) and the `preload_on_switch` checkbox (default off, when to enable, why not by default), plus mentions the daemon-side `OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS` env var as a bonus knob for users with RAM to spare. "Pointing Hearthkin at a remote Ollama" walks through the new Preferences → Connections → Ollama host row — what to type, how to use the Test button, the remote-side `OLLAMA_HOST=0.0.0.0` prerequisite for the daemon to accept network connections, and a network-privacy note about LAN-vs-internet usage.

- **Cross-references woven into existing copy.** The cold-start hint paragraph in "Chatting one-on-one" now points at the new keep_alive option. The "Chat feels slow on first message after idle" troubleshooting entry now mentions both keep_alive (Ollama mitigation) and Cache TTL (Anthropic OpenRouter mitigation). New troubleshooting entry "Hearthkin can't reach my remote Ollama machine" covers the bind-address / firewall / wrong-IP / asleep-machine cause space. The Connections preferences description mentions the new Ollama host row.

- **Kin manual updated** with the design docs added during the v0.4.7 / v0.5.0 work (`kin-migration.md`, `ai-to-ai-async.md`, `multi-kin-rooms-shared-history.md`) that hadn't been listed under "Design documents." The kin-migration doc in particular is the kin's own continuity story — worth them knowing it exists. Cost section gained a brief Ollama-latency paragraph so the kin understands "first reply after a long gap was slow because the daemon unloaded the model" and knows the operator has settings to mitigate it, even though the kin doesn't configure those settings themselves.

## v0.5.2 — 2026-06-01

Ollama-side work that's been deferred while OpenRouter/Anthropic concerns took most of the recent attention. Three independent additions, all opt-in with safe defaults, scoped together as "Phase 1" of getting Ollama ready for the operator's August Mac arrival (remote-Ollama topology: Mac runs the daemon, Windows runs Hearthkin).

### Added

- **Per-kin `keep_alive` setting (Settings → Model && generation → Ollama-only options).** Ollama's daemon unloads a model from memory after 5 minutes of idle by default — fine for occasional use, painful when you talk to a kin every 10-15 minutes and pay the 20-60s cold-load on every reply. New dropdown with four options: "Ollama default (5 minutes)" / "30 minutes" / "1 hour" / "Until I close Ollama (never unload)". Empty value (the default) sends nothing to the daemon, preserving exact historical behavior — no change for existing kin. Plumbed through `llm_backend.chat()` → streaming and blocking Ollama paths + raw HTTP fallback. Callers don't need to pass it explicitly: `chat()` looks the kin's setting up from config when `kin_name` is provided, same lazy-import pattern `_resolve_image_history_keep` already uses. No effect on OpenRouter-routed kin (the parameter is silently ignored).

- **Per-kin `preload_on_switch` checkbox (same Settings tab).** When on, switching TO that kin in the UI fires a background `/api/chat` with model-only — Ollama starts loading the model into memory while the user is reading chat history. By the time they send their first message, the model's already warm. **Default OFF** because Hearthkin is a public program with users across the hardware spectrum: a CPU-inference user on a tight system doesn't want every kin-click triggering an eviction/load cycle if they were just browsing. Users with headroom (real GPU, lots of RAM, or the operator's Mac unified memory in August) opt in for the kin they use heavily. The warm-up honors the kin's `keep_alive` so the load window matches what chat will use. Silent on failure — best-effort; the user's first real send will produce the actionable error if Ollama isn't reachable. No-op for OpenRouter kin.

- **Remote Ollama host (Preferences → Connections → "Ollama host").** Hearthkin can now point at an Ollama daemon on another machine. Text field for the host URL, Test button that round-trips `/api/tags` against the currently-typed value (not the saved one — lets the user verify reachability + model count before committing) and reports via status field + NVDA speech. Empty value = use `OLLAMA_HOST` env var or default localhost (exact historical behavior). Implemented as `llm_backend.set_ollama_host()` module-level override that `_resolve_ollama_host()` reads, plus `_ollama_chat_callable()` that constructs `ollama.Client(host=...)` for the streaming/blocking paths (module-level `ollama.chat()` doesn't accept `host=`). Frame calls `set_ollama_host()` at startup from app config so chat + capability detection + model list + startup probe all target the same daemon. Threaded through every direct-HTTP path: raw chat fallback, both `_ollama_show_raw` copies (llm_backend's and model_utils'), `get_models` (uses `ollama.Client` when overridden), and the startup probe. The "Ollama not detected" startup advisory now adapts its message when the host is remote — different cause space (remote machine asleep / wrong IP / wrong port) vs the local "Ollama not installed" case.

### Fixed

- **Saved configuration was the only way to point Hearthkin at a non-default Ollama daemon, and even then incompletely.** Before this release, `OLLAMA_HOST` set as an env var was honored by the ollama-python client's chat calls and by `_ollama_chat_raw`'s fallback path — but `_ollama_show_raw` (in two places), the model list, and the startup probe all hardcoded `localhost:11434`. So a user pointing chat at a remote daemon would silently get "no capability info" for every model (capability detection falling back to None), an empty model list, and a misleading startup error message. Now all five paths flow through the same resolver. Also: there was no in-app UI for the env var, which made it invisible to non-CLI users (i.e. the median Hearthkin user).

## v0.5.1 — 2026-06-01

Hotfix for a regression introduced in v0.5.0's `cache_ttl` plumbing. Same-day turnaround — the bug shipped this morning, surfaced through a cron-failure Telegram notification this afternoon, fixed this evening.

### Fixed

- **`NameError: name 'cache_ttl' is not defined` on every desktop send and every room send.** v0.5.0 added a per-kin `cache_ttl` field and plumbed it through every conversational surface, but the four worker methods that actually make the LLM call on the desktop/room paths (`_run_streaming_inline`, `_run_tool_loop_inline`, `_run_room_streaming_inline`, `_run_room_tool_loop_inline`) referenced `cache_ttl` at the `llm_backend.chat(...)` / `run_tool_loop(...)` call sites without taking it as a parameter — the outer method's local doesn't leak into a method called from inside a worker thread closure. Every desktop and room send today raised NameError, caught by the worker's `except Exception`, painted as `[error: name 'cache_ttl' is not defined]` in chat. The Telegram path (DM, group, tool-loop) and the cron isolated subprocess were unaffected because they define `cache_ttl` correctly within their own function scopes. Cron's active-kin inject path routes through `_send_message` and therefore tripped the bug — which is how the operator noticed: the v0.4.6 cron-failure-notify pipeline relayed Ash's noon wake-up failure to Telegram. Fix: added `cache_ttl="auto"` as a keyword-only parameter on all four methods, threaded `cache_ttl=cache_ttl` through both worker dispatchers. The "auto" default matches the rest of the codebase, so any future caller that forgets to pass it still produces a working (provider-default-TTL) request rather than a NameError.

## v0.5.0 — 2026-06-01

Minor version bump because the **memory rearchitecture** is a real structural shift, not a patch. Distillation no longer writes to memory.md — it writes to a per-scope staging area the kin reads during nightly tending and decides what becomes canonical memory. Auto-consolidation is disabled. The kin is the arbiter of their own memory; nothing automatic rewrites it. Designed in the v0.4.6-era `docs/design/memory-architecture-and-ritual-framing.md` doc, informed by new research into how seven companion-AI platforms handle this (`docs/design/companion-ai-memory-research-2026-06-01.md`), implemented and shipped same day.

Plus: per-kin cache TTL UI (Anthropic 1h opt-in), context-bar honesty fix on the desktop UI to match v0.4.7's `context_status` tool fix, kin manual at `~/.hearthkin/kin_manual.md` for the kin to read on demand, `read_file` line-range support so large files like Ash's 2.2 MB conversation.jsonl are reachable, and a cross-surface empty-reply salvage path that fixes the Haiku 4.5 + `note` tool "tool was the action" silence pattern on Telegram DM, Telegram group, desktop, and rooms.

### Added

- **Memory architecture v2: staging area + kin as arbiter + tending ritual.** Distillation now writes to `~/.hearthkin/agents/<kin>/staging/<scope>.md` (one file per surface — desktop, each non-shared Telegram DM, each non-shared Telegram group).
