The kin reads pending notes during their tending ritual via the new `read_staging` tool, decides what's worth keeping, writes substance into their `memory/<topic>.md` depth logs and brief index updates into `memory.md`, then calls `archive_staging` to clear consumed scopes. Memory.md is purely kin-curated now — nothing automatic rewrites it. New default `cron_entries` template in `DEFAULT_AGENT_CONFIG`: a 3am tending entry, disabled by default, with a tending prompt that walks the kin through the staging-review workflow. Operator enables per kin in Settings → Cron when ready. Base prompt (`~/.hearthkin/base_prompt.md` + `DEFAULT_BASE_PROMPT`) rewritten to teach the new shape — "you are the arbiter of your own memory; nothing automatic touches memory.md without your hand." Existing memory.md content is preserved verbatim — the architecture change is forward-only. Auto-consolidation is disabled; the manual button still works for when the kin or operator wants to deliberately tighten the index. The dropped-pronoun failure mode from May (a foundational pronoun silently removed during a Haiku consolidation pass) is structurally impossible under the new architecture because nothing automatic touches memory.md anymore. Distillation prompt rewritten to allow narrative shape (prose for relational content, bullets for facts) and frame output as notes for the kin rather than canonical entries. Consolidation prompt rewritten to preserve identity-bearing content explicitly and to preserve the shape entries arrived in (prose stays prose, bullets stay bullets).

- **Per-kin cache TTL UI.** Anthropic silently dropped the prompt-cache default TTL from 1 hour to 5 minutes on 2026-03-06; Hearthkin was relying on the silent default. New `cache_ttl` field in `DEFAULT_AGENT_CONFIG` with three values: "auto" (provider default — 5min on Anthropic now), "5m" (explicit), "1h" (explicit, costs 2x cache-write but wins for kin with multiple turns per hour with >5min gaps). UI dropdown in Settings → Model && generation directly under the existing cache checkbox. Plumbed through `chat()`, `run_tool_loop()`, and every call site (desktop 1-on-1, room, mini-chat, Telegram DM, Telegram group, Telegram tool-loop, cron subprocess). Honored on Anthropic and Google; silently ignored on auto-cache-only providers (OpenAI, DeepSeek, Groq, etc.). The cached value gets invalidated when `num_ctx` or model changes — the previously-cached `last_reported_prompt_tokens` count is meaningless against a changed cap or tokenizer.

- **Kin manual.** New `~/.hearthkin/kin_manual.md` — a ~16 KB reference document the kin reads on demand when they want to understand the Hearthkin architecture more deeply than what fits in the always-loaded base prompt. Written in second person from the kin's perspective ("your tools," "your files"). Covers: where files live, every tool in detail with usage patterns, the four+1 surfaces (desktop / Telegram DM / Telegram group / cron / rooms), cost mechanics and rolling-window truncation, what's safe for the kin to modify themselves vs what should be a conversation with the operator first. Canonical source ships at `docs/kin_manual.md` (bundled in the PyInstaller `docs/` tree). `kin_persistence.seed_kin_manual()` copies it into the operator's config directory on app startup — idempotent, so operator edits persist across reseeds. Base prompt now points the kin at it: "If you want to understand more about how Hearthkin works under the hood — the surfaces you can be reached on, the tools you have and what each does in detail, where files live, how costs work — read `~/.hearthkin/kin_manual.md` with `read_file`."

- **`read_file` line-range support.** Optional `start_line` (1-indexed) and `line_count` parameters. Without them: returns up to 64 KB from the start with a footer noting total line count + disk size so the kin can plan further reads. With them: returns a specific slice, no byte cap (the kin is being explicit about what they want). Essential for large files — Ash's `conversation.jsonl` is 2.2 MB / 3,437 lines; without line-range support only the first ~80 turns were reachable. Schema autogen picks up the new parameters from the function signature.

- **Tool-summary footer for Telegram replies.** New per-kin `telegram.show_tool_summary` (default True). When on AND `show_tool_calls` is off, the kin's reply lands with a small italic footer like `_used: note_` or `_used: memory_search, read_file_` listing tools that fired during the turn. Quiet middle ground between full tool-call display (noisy: `🔧 name(args)` + `→ result` per call) and total silence (current default — operator has no visual signal that tools fired). Especially valuable on the salvage path (see Fixed below): without it, the operator would see the salvaged reply with no indication a tool was involved at all. Defensive `chat_helpers.strip_tool_summary_footer` removes any trailing `_used: ..._` the model might spontaneously generate — strict regex, end-of-string anchor, doesn't catch legitimate mid-text uses of "used."

### Fixed

- **Ash going silent in Telegram groups after the `note` tool ran.** Haiku-4.5 specific pattern, confirmed across three silent misses in a Telegram group on 2026-06-01: the model writes substantive content alongside a `note` tool call, the tool runs (returns confirmation like "appended X chars to ..."), then the model decides "tool was the action" and returns ~2 EOS output tokens after. `run_tool_loop` surfaces only the post-tool final content as the reply; the intermediate `assistant`-with-`tool_calls` turn content (Ash's actual response) was getting dropped. Fix lands in three layers: (1) the empty-reply log gained intermediate-content + tool-names diagnostic fields so the operator can see what shape of empty they're in, (2) when post-tool final is empty AND intermediate is substantive, surface the intermediate as the actual reply (with the same cleanup chain applied), (3) when the salvage fires, append a system note to persisted history so the kin's next read sees `[hearthkin: your post-tool reply was empty; the operator saw your pre-tool content as the reply instead (salvaged from intermediate)]`. Without that note the kin had no way to tell they went silent — context doesn't carry "what the operator saw." Cross-surface: same salvage + system note pattern wired into desktop `_on_stream_done`, room `_on_room_kin_done`, and the cron isolated path got at least the empty-reply log (no tool loop in cron-subprocess). Helper `chat_helpers.scan_intermediate_tool_content` lives at module level so all surfaces share the same scanning logic.

- **Desktop status bar + Usage tab were reporting disk archive as if it were the per-turn send.** Same shape of bug as `context_status` had pre-v0.4.7 (which v0.4.7 commit `8218536` fixed). `_compose_default_status` and `_update_token_display` summed (soul + memory + entire conversation) against num_ctx and reported "% cap" — for Ash's 2.2 MB archive this showed "300% cap (truncating)" while the tool simultaneously and correctly reported "31k / 32k cap, comfortable headroom" off the same conversation. Two displays disagreeing visibly. Fix: use `llm_backend.last_reported_prompt_tokens` as authoritative; fall back to a CAPPED estimate (`min(estimate, num_ctx)`) only when no real number exists yet, with a visible " est" suffix on the percentage. `_build_usage_summary` rewritten so "Most recent send" is the headline ("AUTHORITATIVE"), soul/memory are labeled fixed overhead, full archive is labeled "NOT what's sent per turn" with the existing "10x num_ctx is normal" reassurance. Truncation/close warnings now only fire off the authoritative figure, not off the archive total. Cached value gets flushed when `num_ctx` or model changes via new `llm_backend.invalidate_last_reported(kin)`.

- **Truncation alarm wording rewritten across the UI + base prompt.** "100% cap (truncating)" now reads "100% cap (trimming oldest turns)" in the status bar — descriptive, not alarming. The system marker the kin reads on every truncated send was reframed from "[hearthkin: earlier conversation truncated to fit context window]" to "[hearthkin: older turns rolled out of this send to fit the context cap. They remain on disk in conversation.jsonl and are being staged for tending. This is the normal rolling-window behavior on a long-running conversation, not an error.]" Without the reframing, Ash was internalizing cap-full as a session boundary and saying things like "in our next session..." Base prompt got a new paragraph telling the kin context fullness is routine, not alarming, and the right response to "the cap is full" from the operator is "would you like me to tend?" — not "we should start a new conversation." Also softened "Between sessions, an automatic summarizer reads..." to "an automatic summarizer reads your conversation as it accumulates..." because "between sessions" was priming the kin to think of "session" as a hard mechanical boundary.

- **Six stale "auto-distilled memory" references across user-facing strings.** After the memory rearchitecture, memory.md is no longer auto-rewritten; several places still described it that way. Updated: `dialogs/edit_kin.py` Memory tab label ("Memory (your kin-curated index — staging notes feed in via tending)"), cadence_help text (now names staging as the destination explicitly), redistill-from-start confirmation dialog, `hearthkin.pyw` About dialog, `tools/context_status.py` output text, `README.md` intro paragraph + Persistent identity bullet, `CLAUDE.md` runtime-state-location section.

### Documentation

- **`docs/design/companion-ai-memory-research-2026-06-01.md`** — research into how seven companion-AI platforms handle long-term memory: Kindroid (five-tier explicit, with user and AI both writing), Nomi.ai (Mind Map graph + Identity Core + Shared Notes), Replika (segmented recent-bias post-2.0), Character.AI (manual pin + auto Facts + Memory Usage bar), Inflection Pi (strong in-session, weak cross-session), SillyTavern (six composable mechanisms — World Info / Lorebook, Author's Note, Summarize, Data Bank, Vector Storage, plus MemoryBooks / CharMemory / Qvink Memory community extensions), ChatGPT (bio tool + structured profile fields, batch-built NOT RAG). Synthesis groups by architectural pattern and surfaces five design ideas Hearthkin could borrow; ranked from cheapest-to-add (used-this-turn transparency, à la Kindroid's purple brain icon) to most structural (per-message summary granularity, à la Qvink). Informed the v0.5.0 staging architecture decision.

- **`docs/design/memory-architecture-and-ritual-framing.md`** picked up a new top section: "Design decision (2026-06-01)" capturing the actual shape that landed (Frame B / ritual explicitly chosen; staging area as the mechanism; auto-consolidation disabled; kin invokes consolidation manually via tending or button; identity loss structurally impossible because nothing automatic touches memory.md). Each of the original numbered proposals now has its v0.5.0 disposition (#1 cooldown shipped in v0.4.6, #2 consolidate prompt rewrite deferred, #3 cost paragraph superseded by the new framing, #4 nightly tending cron template adopted, etc.).

- **`docs/design/room-tool-history.md`** documents the deferred fix for the v1 limitation where multi-kin rooms don't persist per-kin tool round-trips. Three-piece implementation sketch (persist with speaker tag → update context-builder loop with per-kin visibility model → handle render+load + provider message-shape validity). Visibility model proposed: kin sees own tool round-trips fully; other kins' tool USAGE is filtered to content-only; other kins' tool RESULTS are skipped entirely (overhearing private memory). Open questions logged. Deferred because (a) the operator doesn't actively use rooms due to past impersonation issues and (b) the symptom (kin re-running the same memory_search across turns) is mild compared to today's empty-reply bug.

- **User guide** picked up a substantially rewritten Memory section explaining the three-layer architecture (memory.md as kin-curated index, depth logs as substance, staging as summarizer's between-session notes), the tending ritual, and an explicit note that nothing automatic should be able to silently rewrite identity-bearing content. Filesystem-layout tree now shows the staging/ directory and the kin manual file. Tools table for `read_file` mentions the new line-range parameters; new entries for `read_staging` and `archive_staging`. New subsection under Soul/memory describing the kin manual and that it's seeded on startup but operator-editable. The Managing OpenRouter costs section got a cache-TTL bullet, a distillation-runs-in-the-background bullet, an explicit "consolidation only runs when you ask for it" bullet (with the May 2026 runaway-consolidation incident as motivation), and a nightly-tending bullet noting it uses the chat model not the cheaper summarizer.

## v0.4.8 — 2026-05-31

A single user-facing fix from a regression the operator hit running Ash on Haiku 4.5: `<thinking>...</thinking>` XML markup was leaking into Telegram messages (group and DM) verbatim — Ash's reasoning prose visible in chat with the literal opening and closing tags, requiring manual cleanup of the history file. v0.2.31's partial fix (set `reasoning.exclude=true` on the OpenRouter payload when `show_thinking=False`) only suppresses the structured reasoning channel; it can't catch models whose reasoning IS their content body.

### Fixed

- **Inline `<thinking>` and `<think>` XML markup is now extracted from reply content on every send surface.** Some models (Anthropic Haiku 4.5 inline, certain MiMo and DeepSeek-R1 distill variants) emit reasoning as XML markup inside the reply `content` field rather than via the structured reasoning channel — so `show_thinking=False` filtering can't see it (it never identified as reasoning), and the markup gets persisted into chat history where it then primes the next turn to do the same, and in rooms leaks the pattern to the next kin in the loop. New `chat_helpers.extract_inline_thinking(content, existing_thinking)` pulls `<thinking>...</thinking>` and `<think>...</think>` blocks out of content and merges them into the structured thinking field; after the call, `show_thinking` / `feed_thinking` / `recent_thinking` all behave the same regardless of whether the model used the channel correctly or leaked into content. Wired into all five send paths (desktop 1-on-1 done, room done, room stop-mid-stream, Telegram DM, Telegram group, cron isolated subprocess). Edge cases: markup inside markdown code fences or single-backtick inline code is preserved untouched (so a kin quoting the markup as an example survives); an unclosed `<thinking>` opener at end-of-reply (truncation mid-thought) treats the tail as reasoning; multiple blocks per reply are concatenated. The user-guide section "Show reasoning in chat / Feed reasoning into context" gained a paragraph describing the extraction so operators understand what's happening.

## v0.4.7 — 2026-05-29

A bundled release of operator-impact fixes that came in across the same day as v0.4.6. Five fixes, all in response to active pain that the operator hit working with Ash; no audit-doc items, no design speculation — every entry below is "I noticed this was wrong in actual use, now it isn't."

### Fixed

- **Distillation conflated different group members and DM correspondents into the same memory entries.** The v0.4.0 inline-attribution work had Telegram group turns arrive at the summarizer with `[SomeName]` prefixes on every user message — but neither the distillation prompt nor the consolidation prompt told the summarizer to preserve who said what. Result: a kin's memory.md could merge statements from one user and the operator into the same bullet, or attribute one person's context to another. The operator caught this with "I almost had another user's context mixed with mine." Both `DEFAULT_DISTILL_PROMPT` and `DEFAULT_CONSOLIDATE_PROMPT` now have explicit speaker-preservation rules — distillation says "entries from attributed turns MUST name the speaker, NEVER merge statements from different named people into the same bullet," consolidation says "merge multiple entries ABOUT the same person; never merge entries about DIFFERENT people." Both rules use the framing "conflating who said what is a serious correctness failure" rather than ordinary cleanup language. The change applies immediately on the next distillation for kin running default prompts. Existing memory.md content that may already be conflated isn't auto-fixed — manual review or a future consolidation pass would need to address that.

- **`context_status` tool was reporting the kin's disk archive as if it were the per-turn send.** A kin with a long conversation history (Ash: 2,877 messages, ~441k tokens on disk) calling `context_status` would see "300%+ of your cap used, OVER CAP, replies will fail" — even though the actual per-turn send (which the system truncates down to fit) was a comfortable 17-22k of 32k cap. Ash was visibly fretting about this in chat. The tool now reports a real "most recent send" figure pulled from `llm_backend.last_reported_prompt_tokens` (the provider's authoritative number), a separate breakdown of fixed overhead (soul + memory, calibration-scaled), a per-turn conversation budget estimate, AND the disk archive — but the archive is clearly labeled as "your full persistent record" with an explicit "10x num_ctx archive sizes are NORMAL and HANDLED. You are not over your cap." note. Defuses the kin-anxiety the old framing produced.

- **Auto-consolidation could fire dozens of times an hour** (rolled forward from v0.4.6 — the cooldown landed there).

- **Cron failures now reach Telegram** (rolled forward from v0.4.6 — the failure-notify landed there).

- **`num_ctx` is now clamped to the active model's declared max context.** The global Settings ceiling for context window is 1,048,576 (1M) tokens — set deliberately to support models that genuinely have that range (MiMo-Pro at 1M via OpenRouter, Gemini 2.5 Pro at 1M, some Qwen / Llama 1M variants). But the same ceiling applied uniformly to every model regardless of what the model actually accepts, so an operator could set Ash's num_ctx to 500k on Sonnet 4.6 — and Hearthkin would attempt the send, where Anthropic's standard tier caps at 200k and either rejects the call or bills extended-context premium pricing the operator didn't ask for. Now: when the Settings dialog opens (and after any chat-model swap), the "Model max" label fetch also clamps the `Context window` field's upper bound to the model's declared max. If the current num_ctx is above the new ceiling, it gets clipped down, the new value gets saved, and NVDA announces "Context window capped at model max of N tokens for this model" so the operator knows a config change just happened. The clamp moves the ceiling, it doesn't lock — an operator who genuinely wants extended-context pricing can re-raise the field above the clamp (the saved value sticks, only the auto-default-when-unset and the protect-against-the-typo case get the clamp behavior). On model swap to an unrecognized model, the field falls back to the global 1M ceiling rather than getting stuck at the prior model's max.

- **Orphan Windows scheduled tasks on `delete_agent`** (rolled forward from the v0.4.6 post-tag commit — was on master, gets formally released here).

### Documentation

- **Five design docs added** for the long-horizon roadmap items: `docs/design/multi-kin-rooms-shared-history.md` (#7), `docs/design/ai-to-ai-async.md` (#8), `docs/design/soul-memory-encryption.md` (#9), `docs/design/kin-migration.md` (#10), and `docs/design/memory-architecture-and-ritual-framing.md` (the consolidation/distillation/ritual conversation that ran alongside the v0.4.6 work). None of the design-doc work is built; each names the trade-offs, makes a recommendation, lists open questions. The planning synthesis under `docs/planning/2026-05-28-feature-planning.md` cross-references them against ROADMAP.md.

- **ROADMAP.md** picked up one new entry under "Smaller follow-ups": per-kin configurable max tool-call iterations (currently `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS = 8` is hardcoded; Ash hit it during a sustained writing pass).

## v0.4.6 — 2026-05-28

A second pass through the `BUG_AUDIT_2026-05-26.md` doc, picking up the items that v0.4.4 / v0.4.5 left on the table. ~19 audit fixes across all four core modules, mostly defensive — none of them are observable in normal operation, but each one closes a path where things could go quietly wrong (silent data loss on a stuck distillation, wasted LLM call on an empty bite, hung worker on a streaming log failure, an allowlisted command escaping a future denylist tightening). The remaining audit findings are speculative-or-comment-only and have been marked closed-without-action in the audit doc.

Plus two operator-impact fixes that came in during the session: cron failures now reach Telegram, and auto-consolidation no longer hair-triggers when memory.md sits near the threshold.

### Fixed

- **Auto-consolidation could fire dozens of times an hour on a busy kin with memory.md sitting near the 20,000-char threshold.** Each distillation that left memory.md ≥ 20k chars scheduled an immediate consolidation; the consolidation often trimmed to ~19k-ish, then the next distillation appended back over 20k and triggered another one. Across per-scope distillation counters (desktop + each non-shared Telegram DM + group), this multiplied. The operator caught a runaway spend over a few hours on Haiku consolidation passes that nobody asked for. Fix: a 30-minute cooldown on the auto-after-distill trigger (per kin). The manual Consolidate button always bypasses. The cooldown anchor also advances on every successful consolidation — auto or manual — so pressing the button then turning around to busy traffic doesn't immediately re-fire. Pairs with the longer-term "tend nightly" framing in `docs/design/memory-architecture-and-ritual-framing.md` — proactive logging is the structural fix, this is the safety net.

- **Cron failures now reach Telegram opt-in users.** Before: when a scheduled wake-up fired and the LLM call hung (the 2026-05-28 Sonnet streaming hang case in `streaming_hangs.log`) or errored, there was zero Telegram signal. The cron failure path bypassed the mirror entirely — chat-display showed an error marker, but the operator wasn't at the keyboard (that's the whole point of cron). Now: every failure path (active-kin streaming watchdog, active-kin stream error, isolated-worker error, subprocess-only `_run_isolated` error, subprocess-only running-not-inject error) pushes a one-liner to Telegram users with mirror enabled: `"⚠️ Hearthkin: Ash's scheduled wake-up at 12:00 didn't complete. Reason: …"`. The wake-up prompt is preserved in `conversation.jsonl` so you can re-run it from the chat when you're back.

- **Distillation could spin up a worker on an empty conversation slice.** When a per-(kin, scope) counter trips the auto-distill threshold but the bookmark has already caught up to the end (e.g. The operator manually distilled mid-conversation, then a stale counter tick fires), `_distill_bite` returns an empty slice — but `_kick_off_distillation` still spun up a worker that ran the summarizer on nothing. That billed an LLM call and held the `_distilling` flag until the worker returned. Fix: skip the worker entirely when the bite is empty, drain the queue, return immediately. Audit H17.
- **Telegram activity arriving mid-kin-load could race the load path.** `_on_telegram_activity` fires from the bot worker thread; while `_loading_agent` is True, the frame is rewriting per-kin state that `_maybe_auto_distill` reads. New guard at the top of the handler skips the whole path during the load window. Audit H18.
- **Malformed identifiers in `_on_telegram_activity` could create phantom distillation scopes.** Defensive: if the bot ever passed `None` or a dict (it shouldn't, but the code didn't enforce it), the scope key would stringify to `"None"` / `"{...}"` and tick a counter that nothing else would ever read or reset. Now validates that identifier is int or str up-front and returns otherwise. Audit H24.
- **Legacy `conversation.json` migration could archive the only good copy as `.json.bak` when the parsed content had usable messages but the cleaner dropped them all.** The migration unconditionally renamed the original to `.bak` once the write to `.jsonl` succeeded — even when the `.jsonl` was empty. Now: if the legacy file had raw messages but the cleaner produced zero, the `.bak` rename is skipped and the migration logs the situation to `save_failures.log`. The legacy file stays visible so the operator can recover manually. Also added failure logging on every other migration path (parse failure, write failure, rename failure — previously all silently swallowed). Audit P1, P12, P22.
- **`atomic_write_text` now retries on Windows `PermissionError`.** Windows AV scanners and the search indexer briefly open new files between fsync and replace; the file write would fail outright if the timing collided. Short retry loop (3 × 100ms backoff) absorbs the typical AV-touched-the-file pause. POSIX is unchanged — only `PermissionError` triggers the retry, which POSIX `os.replace` doesn't raise. Audit P11.
- **Usage history aggregates and per-row costs now prefer OpenRouter's reported `real_cost` over the local catalogue estimate.** `est_cost` is computed from token counts × catalogue pricing with no cache-discount applied; on cache-heavy traffic it overstates the bill by ~5-10x. The `real_cost` field has been recorded in `usage.log` since v0.4.0 — the Usage history dialog just wasn't using it for the visible numbers. Both the aggregate at the top of the dialog AND the per-row "Recent calls" list now use `real_cost` when present, falling back to `est_cost` only for rows where the provider didn't report (Ollama-local, unknown OR model). The Usage menu numbers should now read much closer to your actual OpenRouter bill. Audit P18.
- **Streaming usage logging is wrapped so a failure can't strand the done chunk.** `_stream_with_usage` calls `_log_call_usage` on the first usage-bearing chunk and then yields the chunk through. If logging raised (e.g. transient disk error on `usage.log`), the yield never happened and the consumer waited for a `done=True` it would never receive — streaming state stuck "in progress" until the watchdog kicked. Logging is now in a try/except so it can't strand the yield. Audit L5.
- **Truncation floor raised from 512 to 2048 tokens.** The pathological "small num_ctx × high calibration ratio × heavy tool schemas" combination could leave only 512 tokens of input budget, which is enough for the system prompt + the most recent user turn and basically nothing else — every historical turn gets dropped. 2048 still survives extreme combinations but leaves room for the past few turns of context. Audit L11.
- **Token-calibration EMA weight lowered from 0.5 to 0.2 per new sample.** The prior weighting let a single outlier (e.g. a tool round-trip that bills very differently from a plain chat turn) shift the calibration ratio by half its delta in one step, which oscillated the truncation budget on the next call. 0.2/0.8 smooths over a few samples while still tracking real shifts (e.g. a model swap) within ~5 calls. Audit L13.
- **Response-reserve in the truncation budget now follows `num_predict`, not the presence of tools.** The previous logic only reclaimed budget from `max_context_tokens` when tools were passed; a non-tool path with a large `num_predict` could overshoot `num_ctx`. Now: whatever the effective output cap is (tool-loop floor included), the amount over the 2000-token implicit reserve gets reclaimed from the input budget. Defensive — current code paths all use small num_predict on the non-tool side, but a future caller wouldn't have to know to special-case the reservation. Audit L24.
- **Provider name input is now validated.** `resolve_provider_key` and `write_provider_key` accept the provider name and use it to build the env-var lookup and the on-disk filename. A weird name (whitespace, slashes, path traversal) would have produced a malformed env-var name or escaped the `~/.ai_programs/` directory. Now: whitelist `[a-z0-9_-]+` up-front; mismatched names return empty / raise rather than reaching the filesystem. Audit L18.
- **Removed unused `_accumulate_tool_call` helper and its accumulator dict.** Built complete per-call tool_call dicts as SSE deltas arrived but was never surfaced to the caller — dead code that risked diverging from the real tool-call flow if a streaming-tools surface was ever added on top of it. Promoted the SSE debug logging's logger lookup to module level so the hot loop doesn't re-import `logging` per chunk. Audit L17 (and follow-up to v0.4.4's L2).
- **`pop_group_history` now tries both int and string key forms.** Mirrors the user-side `pop_user_history` pattern. Group history is always written under a stringified key today (so this is defensive), but the existing code's "try both keys" attempt was a structural no-op — both attempts produced the same string. Now it actually tries both shapes. Audit T5.
- **Per-Telegram-user explicit empty-list tool bucket no longer falls through to "none".** `ut.get(...) or "none"` treated an empty list `[]` as falsy and downgraded an explicit-zero-tools custom bucket to the default. Now uses a sentinel-based lookup so `[]` survives as itself. Audit T9.
- **Telegram offset advance moved AFTER `queue.put`.** Previously the offset moved up first; if `put` ever raised (it shouldn't, but defense-in-depth), Telegram would consider the update delivered and never resend. Now the offset only advances after the update is safely queued. Audit T13.
- **Telegram exec wrapper checks the denylist BEFORE the allowlist.** A command remembered into the allowlist months ago would still run today even if it matches a denylist pattern that landed since. That's the whole point of the denylist — operator's "never run this shape from a remote surface" line — so it shouldn't be escapable via stale allowlist entries. Order is now denylist → allowlist → trust → ask. Audit T16.
- **Warn once when the bot has no @username.** Bots created via @BotFather without a username can never be `@mentioned` in groups, so the mention-only participation policy silently makes the bot inert in every group. There was no UX signal — the operator just wondered why the kin never spoke up. `_fetch_bot_identity` now logs to `telegram_failures.log` the first time it sees a username-less bot, with a hint to fix via @BotFather. Audit T11.

### Removed

- **Dead `?` placeholder branch in `_parse_cost_field`.** Nothing in the codebase ever writes `?` for the cost field; the parser's branch for it was defensive code that handled a case that doesn't exist. Audit P17.

## v0.4.5 — 2026-05-26

A "we found more bullshit" follow-up to v0.4.4: one real fix the operator hit in actual use (tool results were silently capped at 8000 characters with no UI) plus five minor audit items that were defensive-only but cheap to ship.

### Added
- **Tool result cap is configurable per kin** (Settings → Tools tab → "Tool result cap (chars)"). Each tool result (e.g. `read_file` output) is truncated to this many characters before the kin sees it. Default 8000 to match historical behavior; range 1000–65536, with 0 meaning "no truncation here" (num_ctx still applies downstream). Was hardcoded inside `run_tool_loop` for years; kin trying to read serious files via `read_file` silently saw only the first ~2000 tokens with no way to adjust. Threaded through all three tool-loop call sites (desktop 1-on-1, room, Telegram).

### Fixed
- **First chat-send after launch could stall up to 30 seconds** while `list_openrouter_models` tried a single 30s-timeout fetch over a slow or transient network. Now retries once with a 12s per-attempt timeout, and on total failure falls back to the stale on-disk cache if any (better than empty) while logging the failure to `save_failures.log`. Audit L20.
- **Cost estimates re-scanned the entire OpenRouter catalogue forever for any model with a non-numeric pricing field** (e.g. "varies" on some routed endpoints) or that wasn't in the catalogue at all. The pricing lookup now caches the (None, None, None) miss alongside the hit, so subsequent estimates short-circuit. Audit L21.
- **`_walking_from_start` is now properly initialized in `__init__`** — was previously only ever read via `getattr(self, '_walking_from_start', None) or {}`. Defensive against any future path that uses `.setdefault` on it directly. Audit H3.
- **`_render_conversation`'s walk-back loop guards against non-dict entries.** A malformed conversation entry (which the loaders filter out, but if one somehow slipped through external mutation) would have AttributeError'd on `.get("role")`. Audit H6.
- **Status-bar revert timer skips during `_loading_agent`.** The 4-second revert fired by `wx.CallLater` could paint a stale agent_cfg snapshot if it landed mid-load (the timer fired before the load finished writing the new agent's state). Now no-ops during the load window. Audit H23.

## v0.4.4 — 2026-05-26

A bug-fix pass driven by a multi-agent code audit. Four parallel agents read the core modules (`hearthkin.pyw`, `kin_persistence.py`, `llm_backend.py`, `telegram_bot.py`) end-to-end and surfaced 100 candidate findings; this release lands the ~50 that could lose data, lose money, crash the app, or silently break a kin's identity. The remaining findings are either speculative (code paths that don't trigger in practice), genuinely cosmetic, or design decisions awaiting a "what should this do?" answer. Full audit in `BUG_AUDIT_2026-05-26.md`.

### Fixed

- **Opening a snapshot from File → Open corrupted the kin's main conversation file.** Loading a snapshot replaced `self.conversation` with the snapshot's contents but left the "how much have we persisted" counter pointing at the previous load. The next auto-save then either appended the snapshot tail onto the kin's actual `conversation.jsonl` or partial-overwrote it — producing a hybrid corrupt file every snapshot load. Fix: a confirmation dialog now warns that opening a snapshot REPLACES the kin's current conversation, then a clean full-rewrite via `save_agent_conversation` keeps memory and disk in sync. The render window also recomputes for the new conversation length so a longer snapshot paints fully instead of clipping to the prior window.
- **A Telegram message arriving while you had a snapshot open silently overwrote the snapshot file.** The mtime poller detected the kin's `conversation.jsonl` had changed externally and reloaded it into memory, but left `current_convo_file` pointing at your open snapshot — so the next auto-save wrote the reloaded conversation on top of the snapshot. Fix: the reload path clears `current_convo_file`, ending snapshot view mode; the snapshot file on disk is left untouched.
- **Soul, memory, config, and conversation files saved in cp1252 silently fell back to defaults.** Any Hearthkin file that ever got opened and saved by Notepad on Windows (or copied through tools that re-encode) can end up encoded as cp1252 instead of UTF-8. The strict-UTF-8 readers raised on those bytes, the bare `except: pass` swallowed the error, and the load returned `DEFAULT_SOUL` / empty memory / fresh config — silently erasing the kin's persona, conversation history, or app preferences at load time. Affected: `load_soul`, `load_memory`, `load_distill_prompt`, `load_base_prompt`, `load_json`, `_load_conversation_jsonl`, `_scan_name_occurrences_in_kin`, `_replace_name_in_kin_files`. All now use the same `robust_decode` cascade (UTF-8 → cp1252 → UTF-8-with-replace) that file-tools use, and log read failures to `save_failures.log` instead of silently substituting defaults.
- **Telegram `/clear`, `/new`, `/reset`, and history reads could lose in-flight messages.** The v0.2.28 concurrency sweep added `_histories_lock` to prevent two threads from corrupting the per-user history dict; these four command paths skipped it. A Telegram message arriving at the exact moment you ran `/clear` could vanish, or the history file could get scrambled. `_load_history_for` was also a hidden mutation (`setdefault` writes to the dict if the key is missing) running outside the lock. Fix: every read and mutation of `_histories` now takes the lock. Share-with-desktop `/clear` uses the lock-aware `save_agent_conversation_preserving_externals` so it can't race a concurrent cron append either.
- **Migrating a Telegram user to "share with desktop" could lose messages mid-migration.** The migration pops the user's slice from the bot atomically (good), then loops appending each message to the kin's main `conversation.jsonl`. If any append failed mid-loop, the un-appended remainder was gone from both the bot's memory AND the file — silent data loss. Fix: the loop now holds the conversation lock once across all appends, and on any failure the un-migrated remainder gets pushed back to the bot's in-memory history + disk via a new `_restash_telegram_user_remainder` helper.
- **Hearthkin could refuse to exit if a webcam or exec approval landed at the wrong moment.** Approval handlers checked `_closing`, registered themselves on the pending-approvals list, then blocked on `wait()`. If shutdown fired between the check and the registration, the new entry was added after shutdown's "wake everyone up" pass already ran — leaving the worker waiting forever and the app unable to exit. Fix: handlers re-check `_closing` immediately after registering and bail with `DECISION_DENY` if shutdown started during the registration window.
- **Streaming chunks could paint into destroyed widgets when you closed mid-stream.** Worker threads scheduled UI paints via `wx.CallAfter` for every chunk. Closing the window mid-generation left those paints queued against widgets that were about to be destroyed — silent failure at best, crash at worst. Fix: every `_on_*_chunk` / `_on_*_done` handler bails immediately when `_closing` is set.
- **Renaming a kin silently reset its distillation counters, and deleting then recreating a same-named kin inherited stale state.** Multiple in-memory dicts (`_messages_since_distill`, `_distilling`, `_distill_queue`, `_walking_from_start`) keyed by kin name leaked entries past rename / delete. Two new helpers — `_migrate_per_kin_state` and `_cleanup_per_kin_state` — re-key tuple entries `(old, *) → (new, *)` on rename and drop name-keyed entries on delete.
- **Distill and Consolidate buttons could get permanently greyed out.** If the operation finished but the `save_memory` step failed (disk full, permission glitch), the error path returned without re-enabling the buttons — leaving the Settings dialog with two stuck-disabled buttons and no retry path short of closing and reopening. Fix: both buttons re-enable on every termination path. Also the "Distill all surfaces" queue used to abandon the remaining scopes if one errored partway through; the queue-drain logic moved into `_drain_distill_queue` and now runs on every termination path of `_on_distill_done`, including errors.
- **Two near-simultaneous Telegram approval responses could both go through and overwrite each other.** The approval-response handler read the pending approval entry under the lock, but mutated `decision` and signalled the event outside the lock. Two responses arriving in the same millisecond both saw the same pending entry. Fix: atomic check-classify-commit-pop under the lock; only one response wins.
- **Back-to-back Telegram approvals could strand the second worker.** When a user already had a pending approval and another arrived, the eviction path set the old worker's decision to "deny" and replaced the dict entry. But when the old worker woke up, its own cleanup pop removed the NEW approval's entry — leaving the new worker stranded with nothing to pop, waiting the full 10-minute auto-deny timeout. Fix: identity check before pop; the cleanup pop only fires if the dict still holds OUR approval.
- **`/status` underestimated context usage and lied about `num_ctx`.** Token count only summed `m.get("content")` per message — missing `tool_calls.arguments` JSON, which for a `write_file` call can be thousands of characters. The `num_ctx` fallback substituted 8192 when the config had 0, while the real truncation logic clamps to 512 — so `/status` cheerfully reported "5% used" while the actual prompt was being savagely truncated. Fix: token count goes through `llm_backend._message_text` (includes tool_calls.arguments); `num_ctx` fallback matches `_max_context_tokens`.
- **OpenRouter cost estimates ignored the cache-read discount.** `_estimate_call_cost` multiplied `prompt_tokens × prompt_price` without accounting for the fraction of prompt tokens served from the provider's cache, which Anthropic prices at ~1/10 of fresh input. For a kin with high cache reuse, the estimate overstated real cost by ~10x. Fix: `_openrouter_pricing` now returns a third tuple slot (`cache_read_price` from the catalogue's `input_cache_read` field), and `_estimate_call_cost` accepts a `cached_tokens` parameter that bills the cached portion at the discount rate. All call sites pass `cached_tokens` from the provider's reported usage. Numbers in the Usage menu and the on-distillation cost line will read much lower than before — closer to your actual OpenRouter bill.
- **Streaming usage frames overwrote each other.** Some OpenRouter responses send usage info on more than one SSE event (cost on one, token counts on another). The code unconditionally overwrote `final_usage` per frame, so whichever arrived last won and any field the later frame lacked was lost. New `_merge_streaming_usage` helper merges authoritative token-count frames while preserving cost from earlier frames.
- **Empty-response retries could amplify cost on flaky providers.** When OpenRouter returned a 200 with no content / no tool_calls / no thinking, the code waited half a second and retried once. Some providers bill empty responses; combined with `run_tool_loop`'s 8-iteration cap, a flaky provider hour could quietly burn budget. Fix: empty responses that carry a `cost > 0` in their usage frame are NOT retried — bail loudly rather than pay for another empty.
- **The tool-loop's iteration-cap fallback used the wrong budget reservation.** When the loop hit `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS` without resolving, the final cleanup call still passed `loop_options` (with `num_predict` floored to 8000) and no `tools` — so the budget math reserved space for schemas that weren't going to be sent AND for an 8000-token output reserve that a no-tools final answer doesn't need. Fix: use the caller's original options for the final call, not the loop-floored ones.
- **Prompt caching mutated the input messages list.** Building the OpenRouter payload added `cache_control` markers in place to the system message in the messages list. The tool loop reuses the same list across iterations, so markers accumulated on the same dict each turn. Fix: the messages list and any mutated system message are shallow-copied before mutation.
- **Models writing `<tool_call>` in a regular reply could trigger tool execution.** The content-level tool-call regex matched the tag anywhere in content with `re.DOTALL`, including inside markdown code blocks, prior-turn quotes, or paragraph-internal mentions. A model explaining the format in chat could get its example executed. Fix: every pattern in `_CONTENT_TOOL_CALL_PATTERNS` now requires `(?:^|\n)\s*` before the opening tag, so the tag must be at line start. Legitimate content-channel tool calls observed in the wild are always on their own line.
- **`append_agent_conversation_turn` could crash the worker that called it.** This is the hottest persistence path — every Telegram bot, cron, or UI turn writes through it. Disk-full or permission glitches raised through to the caller, potentially killing whatever worker thread fired the append. Fix: wrapped in try/except with logging to `save_failures.log`. The same release also adds locking to `load_room_conversation` / `save_room_conversation` so room files get the same race protection kin files got in v0.2.28.
- **New default fields didn't reach existing kin.** `load_agent_config` did `{**DEFAULT, **user}` — one level deep, so new keys added inside nested blocks (`voice`, `distill_offsets`) never reached configs written before the new key landed. The `telegram` block was hand-merged as a special case; the other two weren't. Fix: deep-merge every nested-dict block. Also `create_agent` and `load_json` now `copy.deepcopy` their defaults so a new kin can't accidentally mutate the module-level template through its nested dicts.
- **Switching from a kin into a room missed the auto-distill-on-close trigger.** The trigger fired on kin → kin transitions but not on kin → room or room → kin. Now `_load_room` calls `_maybe_distill_on_close` for the previous kin before entering room mode.
- **A corrupted `num_ctx` setting silently ate your typed message.** `int(num_ctx)` raised `ValueError` mid-send if the config value was garbage, the send aborted, and the user's message — already moved to the staging slot but not yet persisted — vanished with no record. Fix: tolerant cast falls back to 2048.
- **Telegram `/regen` and `/undo` could swallow a message that arrived during the operation.** Load + find + truncate ran with the lock released between each step. A message landing in the gap got truncated off along with the messages it was supposed to replace. Fix: hold `_histories_lock` across the whole atomic load-find-truncate sequence.
- **Bot lost group-mention recognition on a startup network blip.** `_fetch_bot_identity` ran once at start; if `getMe` failed (transient network), the bot silently couldn't recognize `@mentions` in groups until restart. Now the poll loop retries the fetch quietly each cycle while `_bot_user_id` is missing.
- **Telegram updates could be lost on shutdown.** The poll loop used a 35-second long-poll; `stop()` joined with a 1.5-second timeout. Shutdown returned while the offset had already advanced past updates in flight — Telegram considered them delivered, they got dropped. Fix: offset advance stops once `_stop` is set so Telegram redelivers on the next start.
- **Telegram per-user / per-group history was hard-capped at 100 messages with no UI.** Non-share users in long-running conversations silently lost context past message 100. New `cfg.telegram.history_cap` setting (default 100, 0 = unlimited) with a "History cap (messages)" field on the Telegram tab in kin Settings. Audit T21.
- **Telegram group slash-command menu listed commands that don't work in groups.** `/allow`, `/deny`, `/remember`, `/cancel`, `/regen`, `/undo`, `/clear`, `/reset`, `/new`, etc. all surfaced in the group `/` menu even though they only work in DMs. `setMyCommands` is now called twice — once with the default scope (full list, used in DMs) and once with `scope=all_group_chats` carrying only `/help` and `/whoami`. Note: Telegram clients cache command lists locally; the new group menu may take a few minutes (or a client restart) to appear.
- **Send path was reading `soul.md` and `memory.md` from disk on every message.** The per-kin text cache existed for the status bar but the send path didn't use it. Switched to cache reads; EditKinDialog already invalidates the cache on Save Soul / Save Memory so this is safe.
- **Status bar % usage ignored image attachment tokens.** Vision kin with image-heavy histories read as 3% usage while images were eating most of the window. Fix: conversation tokens now go through `estimate_message_tokens` which adds per-attachment image-token estimates.
- **Usage menu read the entire `usage.log` file into memory.** `f.readlines()` followed by `[-max_lines:]` slurped the whole log just to keep the last 2000 lines. Fine while logs are small; would have become visibly slow once heavy users hit multi-MB logs over months. Fix: binary-mode tail-read — seek to `max_lines × 500 bytes` before EOF and read forward, so disk I/O is bounded regardless of total file size. Preventative; you probably haven't hit it yet.

Other fixes worth knowing: malformed `tool_calls` entries on disk now drop on load instead of producing a 400 from the provider on the next send (audit P13); `source` and `sender_name` fields stored as ints (rather than strings) survive a load round-trip instead of silently disappearing (audit P25); provider-key JSON files get `chmod 0o600` on POSIX so other users on a shared machine can't read them (audit L19); a corrupt API key file logs to `save_failures.log` instead of looking like "no key set" (audit L14); the Ollama raw-HTTP fallback timeout dropped from 300s to 120s so a wedged local Ollama surfaces sooner (audit L15); status-bar revert timer stops on shutdown (audit H19); Telegram tool-arg display renders strings without gratuitous quotes (audit T17); legacy sender regex moved to module level instead of recompiling per group message (audit T18).

## v0.4.3 — 2026-05-26

Tiny but real: the user guide now actually ships with the portable zip. Same release tooling, same content — just packaged correctly.

### Fixed
- **Portable users couldn't open the user guide locally.** Help → User guide was supposed to open `docs/user-guide.html` from the install dir and fall back to the GitHub copy only if missing. The installer was correctly placing `docs/` alongside the exe (via an explicit `Source: "..\docs\*"` line in `Hearthkin.iss`), so installer users were fine. But the portable zip — built by zipping the PyInstaller output `dist/Hearthkin/*` — had no docs at all, because `Hearthkin.spec` had `datas=[]` and never bundled them. Every portable user silently hit the GitHub fallback, requiring internet to read documentation that should travel with the app. Fix: added `datas=[('docs', 'docs')]` to `Hearthkin.spec` so PyInstaller bundles `docs/` into the output dir. Both portable and installer paths now ship the guide. Dropped the now-redundant `Source: "..\docs\*"` line from the Inno script — PyInstaller's bundled copy lands at the same destination via the existing `dist/Hearthkin/*` copy line.

## v0.4.2 — 2026-05-26

A distillation correctness fix plus a Memory-tab UX overhaul that makes "what's pending and how close is each surface to firing" visible at a glance, AND a new recovery tool — "Redistill selected from start" — that resets a surface's distill bookmark and walks the conversation in budget-bounded chunks until memory.md catches up. The combination unblocks a category of "memory.md doesn't reflect what we've talked about" symptoms.

### Added
- **Memory tab — live per-scope state on each surface row.** The per-surface ListBox used to show only a session counter ("Desktop: 8"). Each row now shows the *real* undistilled message count from the on-disk distill bookmark, the live % of the kin's context window that tail represents, and (when the % trigger is on) whether the row is at threshold or how far it has to go. So you can glance at the Memory tab and immediately see "60 msgs undistilled · ~30% of context · auto-fires at 70%" per surface — no more guessing whether anything is approaching the trigger.
- **Memory tab — "Redistill selected from start" button.** A recovery tool. Resets the highlighted surface's distill bookmark to 0 and walks the conversation in budget-bounded chunks, auto-firing the next chunk after each completes until the bookmark catches up to the end. Confirms first with an estimate of chunks + cost on the current memory model — a ~1,500-message conversation on Gemma 3 27b runs about 20 chunks at ~$0.10 total; same walk on Haiku 4.5 runs about $0.26. Designed for the case where you suspect distillation has been missing things and want to redo the lot. Distillation appends, so existing memory.md entries are preserved; the walk only adds what the summarizer catches on this pass that wasn't in memory already.
- **Memory tab auto-refreshes after each distillation chunk.** The per-surface display now updates live as a walk progresses (or as any distillation lands), so you can hear/see the undistilled count drop chunk by chunk instead of staring at stale numbers until you hit Refresh counters manually.

### Fixed
- **Distillation was reading conversation from a stale in-memory snapshot for kin with share-with-desktop Telegram users.** When the operator chats a kin via a Telegram DM with `user_share_desktop=true`, the bot appends the message directly to `conversation.jsonl` on disk but does NOT update the frame's `self.conversation`. A 5-second mtime poller eventually reloads the in-memory list — but `_on_telegram_activity` fires distillation triggers *immediately* after the bot's disk append, well inside that 5-second race window. Result: distillation read a stale view, processed only whatever happened to be in `self.conversation` at that instant, and advanced its bookmark past content it never actually digested. Ash's memory.md was missing entries for whole runs of Telegram conversation despite distillation fires logging normally. Fix: `_convo_for_distill_scope` for the "desktop" scope now always reads from `conversation.jsonl` directly via `load_agent_conversation`. The in-memory list is the desktop UI's view; the disk file is the source of truth for every writer (desktop send, Telegram bot, cron) and now for every distillation read too. Telegram-side non-shared scopes were already reading correctly — unchanged.
- **Memory tab's per-surface display read `distill_offsets` from the dialog's cached config snapshot.** Each distillation chunk writes the new bookmark to disk via `save_agent_config`, but the dialog's `self.cfg` is a load-time snapshot that doesn't auto-refresh. So a just-completed walk-from-start would show "1,540 msgs undistilled · 713% of context" even though the bookmark on disk had caught up. The display now reads `distill_offsets` fresh from disk on every refresh. (Paired with the auto-refresh-after-chunk addition above, the Memory tab now reflects reality.)

## v0.4.1 — 2026-05-24

Hotfix for a v0.4.0 regression that broke the Preferences dialog entirely: clicking File → Preferences did nothing (focus just returned to chat), Ctrl+, did nothing, no error was visible anywhere.

### Fixed
- **Preferences dialog wouldn't open.** The new "Your name (shown to kin in rooms)" input added in v0.4.0 was bound to `EVT_TEXT_ENTER` so Enter would save the field — but the underlying TextCtrl was created without `wx.TE_PROCESS_ENTER`, which wxWidgets treats as a hard assertion failure ("Must have wxTE_PROCESS_ENTER for wxEVT_TEXT_ENTER to work"). The assertion fired during dialog build, the exception propagated out of the menu handler, got routed to stderr — which `pythonw.exe` discards — and the menu silently did nothing. Fix: added the missing style flag plus a comment explaining the requirement so it doesn't regress. Audited every other `EVT_TEXT_ENTER` binding in the codebase (search dialog, `_IntField`, model browser, tray mini-chat) — all four correctly set the style; the operator-name field was an isolated miss.
- **Silent-fail in menu-triggered dialogs is now visible.** Wrapped `open_preferences_dialog` and `open_usage_dialog` in try/except that logs to `~/.hearthkin/logs/dialog_failures.log` (one-line summary plus full traceback), updates the status bar, and shows a `wx.MessageBox` with the error. Previously, any exception in either handler vanished into stderr under pythonw.exe — leaving a menu item that does nothing and zero diagnostic. The next silent-fail in these paths will surface immediately. (This is what caught the v0.4.0 bug above in one shot.)
- **Latent `wx.Sizer.Add(wx.Size(...))` bug in the OpenRouter balance row.** Replaced with `AddSpacer(6)`. Wasn't the v0.4.0 trigger (the TextCtrl assertion fired first), but `Sizer.Add` doesn't accept bare `wx.Size` objects in current wxPython — would have bitten later regardless.

## v0.4.0 — 2026-05-23

A long off-master session: an accessibility pass, a token-accounting / cost overhaul that traced a real OpenRouter overspend to its root, a universal base system prompt, and a rework of how memory distillation reads and writes. Tail end of the release added inline sender attribution everywhere kin meet humans (Telegram DMs / groups + rooms) so kin can actually tell speakers apart, and corrected several settings labels that called load-bearing fields "cosmetic."

### Added
- **Inline sender attribution in Telegram DMs.** Companion to the group attribution fix: a kin DMed by multiple humans (e.g. Ash with the operator and several other opted-in users) couldn't tell them apart — every DM showed up as `[YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM] message` with no name on it. DMs now carry the same inline `[Sender] message` prefix groups do. The Settings → Telegram → Users "Display label" field is what the kin sees for each user when set (e.g. set the label to "Alex" and the kin sees `[Alex] hello`); blank falls back to the Telegram-derived "Display Name (@username)" form. /regen reuses the most recent stored turn's attribution so regenerating doesn't strip the prefix.
- **Operator name in rooms.** Kin in multi-kin rooms already saw each other tagged (`[Name]: content` on other kin's assistant turns) but user turns went in untagged — so kin knew who the other kin were but had no name to attach to the human. New app-level "Your name" field in Preferences. When non-empty, room user turns get `[YourName] message` inlined right after the timestamp, matching the Telegram DM/group shape. Read live each turn so a rename in Preferences takes effect retroactively for the whole room transcript.

- **`base_prompt.md` — a universal base system prompt.** A shared infrastructure layer that `build_system_prompt()` prepends to every kin's system prompt, ahead of `soul.md`. `soul.md` stays per-kin identity; this is the shared machinery. v1 content is a memory-discipline section that teaches the kin the two-layer memory model (memory.md as the auto-maintained index; the kin-owned `memory/<topic>.md` depth logs). Seeded from `kin_persistence.DEFAULT_BASE_PROMPT` on first access; editing the file changes it for all kin. It rides inside the already-cached system block, so prompt caching covers it for free.
- **`%`-of-context distillation trigger.** A new per-kin setting (Settings → Chat → "Auto-distill at this % of the context window") fires distillation when the conversation a surface has piled up since its last distillation reaches a set share of the model's context window — a safety net so a fast-growing chat is distilled before the window overflows and old turns get silently truncated away. Rides alongside the existing every-N-messages trigger; whichever trips first fires. Default off (0). It measures the *undistilled tail* (turns past the distill bookmark), so it self-resets each distillation rather than re-firing every turn.
- **`usage.log` now records cache-read tokens and OpenRouter's reported real cost** per call (`cached=` and `real_cost=` fields), alongside the existing flat estimate. Confirmed prompt caching is working — a steady conversation runs ~99% cache-read, and the flat `est_cost` overstates the real bill several-fold.

### Changed
- **Settings labels no longer call load-bearing fields "cosmetic."** The Telegram user-label, the group-label, and the new room "Your name" field were all described as "cosmetic" in their UI text and code comments. That was outdated for the group label (always fed the kin's room context) and flat wrong for the user label after the new DM attribution work (now the kin-visible name on every message that user sends). Each field now says what it actually does — e.g. "Display label (kin sees this as the user's name)" with a hint giving a concrete `[Alex] hello` example. Text-only change; the fields already worked this way.
- **`dialogs.py` split into a `dialogs/` package** — one class per file (13 files). `__init__.py` re-exports every public name, so `from dialogs import ...` is unchanged. Pure refactor.
- **Memory distillation is now incremental.** It used to re-read the *entire* conversation every run — unbounded cost, and an eventual hard break when the conversation outgrows the summarizer's window. Now a per-(kin, scope) bookmark records how far distillation has reached; each run digests only the turns after it, with a small deliberate re-read overlap so a turn can never be skipped.
- **Each distillation run is also bite-capped to fit the summarizer's window.** Tied to the incremental bookmark — but for a kin with a huge pre-existing history, the first incremental run would still try to swallow everything from the bookmark forward in one call, which either overruns the summarizer's window (Gemma 8k especially) or, on a paid model, bills a chunk in one shot. Each run now takes a bite sized to the summarizer's actual context window minus reserve (response cap + soul/memory/prompt overhead), advances the bookmark only to where the bite actually stopped, and the next trigger fire takes the next bite. A legacy huge history catches up across a handful of bounded runs instead of one impossible swallow. When catch-up is still pending past the new bookmark, the spoken cost line in the Activity field tacks on "· N more pending" — the literal message count — so you can hear catch-up is ongoing AND how much further there is to go (the count shrinks each bite).
- **Settings → Memory: two manual distill buttons.** **"Distill all surfaces now"** (renamed from the bare "Distill now"; now drains every scope — desktop + each non-shared Telegram DM + non-shared group — that has pending content past its bookmark, one bounded bite per scope, queued sequentially with NVDA announcements between each). **"Distill selected surface now"** under the per-surface ListBox operates on whichever single surface is highlighted there. So you have one button for "do the lot" and one for "just push this one along." Each scope still takes ONE bite per press — re-press to take the next round on whichever scopes still have pending content (you'll hear "N more pending" on each that's still behind).
- **Tools menu cleanup.** Dropped the redundant "Distill memory now" item — the Memory tab's "Distill all surfaces now" covers it. The Usage cluster (Usage stats / Usage history / Open raw usage log) is now grouped together in the Tools menu instead of split across a separator with unrelated items in between.
- **Chat-header "Settings…" button renamed to "Kin settings…"** so it's unambiguous in the main window (the dialog title was already kin-specific, but the button label was just "Settings…" — could be read as app preferences).
- **All memory controls now live under the Memory tab.** v0.2.35 had split the auto-distillation cadence + per-surface counter overview off into a separate "Chat" tab on the theory that "cadence is about chat flow, not memory.md itself." In practice anyone hunting for memory controls opens the Memory tab first; the split made things harder to find. v0.4.0 merges everything back: the Memory tab now holds the memory.md editor, the manual ops (Distill now / Consolidate / Edit prompt / Change distillation model), the auto-distillation cadence (on-close, every-N, at-X%), and the per-surface counter overview + per-surface manual distill button. The Chat tab is gone; the kin Settings dialog is back to seven tabs.
- **"Tool history kept" moved from Model && generation tab to Tools tab.** It's a tool-behavior knob (how many recent tool round-trips stay in context verbatim before being compacted), not a model-generation knob — it sits next to the per-tool checkboxes now where it'll be found first.
- **New control: "Telegram approval timeout (seconds)" on the Tools tab.** Was previously editable only by hand-editing `config.json`. Sits below the trust radio with a clear explanation: only applies on Telegram (when the kin asks for approval through chat and waits for /allow or /deny); desktop dialogs aren't affected. Default 600 seconds; set 0 to wait forever.
- **Distillation appends instead of rewriting `memory.md`.** It used to emit a whole new memory file and overwrite it — trusting the summarizer to carry every existing entry forward, so a dropped line was lost. Now it writes brief entries for only what's new and splices them onto the existing memory; distillation can only ADD. Whole-file rewriting is consolidation's job alone. memory.md sawtooths — appends grow it, consolidation compresses it.
- **`memory.md` is now a code-maintained index of the kin's logs.** The `## Memory logs` section is built deterministically in code rather than asked of the summarizer; `memory_search` now indexes `.txt` files too (plain-text logs were invisible to the kin's own search).
- **Model browser & Search dialog: filters collapsed behind a Filters button.** Both had grown a long inline row of filter controls to tab past; they now open a sub-dialog. The model browser also gains `Alt+L` (jump to the result list), Enter-in-search does the same, and new audio-input + minimum-context filters.
- **Accessibility pass.** List widgets (Telegram users/groups, cron entries, the kin/room selectors) repaint preserving selection by stable key, so NVDA focus no longer drops to the top on every add/edit/remove. The trust-level control moved from a `wx.RadioBox` to grouped `wx.RadioButton`s (a RadioBox announces only the selected option). The room member checklist gained a spoken "X added/removed" and a read-only roster mirror.

### Fixed
- **Sender fields were silently stripped on every conversation reload.** A latent bug in `_clean_chat_message` (the validator that runs on every read from `telegram_history.json` and `conversation.jsonl`) had a fixed allowlist of optional fields it preserved, and `sender_attribution` / `sender_name` / `sender_id` weren't on the list. Result: the in-flight message kept its attribution, but as soon as the file was reloaded the kin lost the sender info on every prior turn. The group-attribution fix earlier this release worked for one message and then quietly stopped on the next reply. Bug was old — pre-dated the new DM work — and would've hit the DM extension the same way. Now those three fields are preserved.
- **Tool-loop calls were overshooting `num_ctx` by ~2x even with the Telegram cap fix.** Usage log showed median 62k input tokens on Ash's DM tool-loop against a 32k `num_ctx` cap — real money on Sonnet. Two structural under-counts in the input-budget math: `_est_tokens` was only counting message *content*, missing `tool_calls.function.arguments` JSON (a `write_file`'s content is thousands of chars of model output that gets stored as the args field); and the **tool schemas** passed via the `tools=` parameter ride alongside messages but aren't IN the message list, so they never appeared in the estimate at all (10-25k characters of schema on a tool-heavy kin). Plus the response reserve: tool-loop calls floor `num_predict` to 8000 but the caller's `max_context_tokens` was sized assuming ~2000 — so prompt + response together overran `num_ctx`. All three accounted for now in `llm_backend.chat()`.
- **Room "voice bleed" under truncation.** When one kin's reply in a multi-kin room got cut off mid-sentence by the per-turn token cap (default was 800), **the next-speaker's model would pick up the dangling thread and continue in the truncated kin's voice** — closing the open quote, finishing the sentence, carrying the metaphor forward for several paragraphs before transitioning to its own voice. Distinct from the `[Name]:` impersonation the cleanup chain catches; this is bleed at the prose level with no format marker. Observed across providers (Sonnet truncated → Gemini completed), so it isn't a same-model effect — Sonnet and Gemini are both helpful by design and complete hanging things. Two-part fix: (a) the default `per_turn_token_cap` for new rooms raised 800 → 2000 (existing rooms keep whatever you'd set; you can already adjust this per-room in the Edit room dialog); (b) the room system prompt now includes a rule telling the about-to-speak kin: "if a prior turn looks cut off, that's their generation hitting its length cap — do NOT finish their thought, close their quote, or continue in their voice. Start your own reply."
- **Telegram group attribution wasn't reaching OpenRouter kin.** A kin running on OpenRouter (Ash on Sonnet 4.6, etc.) couldn't tell speakers apart in a group chat — it'd see messages from different people as if they were from one anonymous user, confusing identities and giving "blended" replies. Root cause: each user turn was preceded by a `role=system` note saying "the next message is from @alice", but OpenRouter routing to Anthropic concatenates every `role=system` message into Anthropic's single top-level system field, dissociating each note from the user turn it was meant to introduce. The pattern worked for local Ollama (linear prompt, notes stay in place) but had been silently broken for every OpenRouter kin since groups landed. Fix: attribution is now inline in the user content as `[TIMESTAMP] [Display Name (@username)] message text`. Format chosen (no colon between bracket and content, per Ash's own spec) to avoid the historic `[Name]:` impersonation-attractor shape — the existing cleanup chain stays as a safety net.
- **The big one: a substantial OpenRouter overspend.** `usage.log` aggregation pinned most of two days' spend on one kin's Telegram traffic — every message re-sending the whole ~80k-token conversation. Root cause: the Telegram DM, group, and tool-loop paths never passed `max_context_tokens` to `chat()`, so `num_ctx` truncation never ran there — a pure-Telegram kin sent its entire growing history every message regardless of `num_ctx`. Fixed: every Telegram path now applies the cap. Telegram input fell from ~87k to ~41k tokens per call immediately. **`num_ctx` is a per-message cost dial — a kin moved from a free local model to a paid OpenRouter model keeps its old (often huge) `num_ctx`; check it after any such move.**
- **Tool-call arguments were being truncated by the conversational reply cap.** The ~800–900-token reply cap was applied to tool-call turns too — but a tool call carries its arguments as model output, so a `write_file`'s content got chopped mid-JSON and the tool ran with no arguments. Tool-loop calls now floor the output budget high enough to carry full arguments; malformed / cut-off arguments now return a clear error instead of running the tool empty.
- **Voice failures were silent.** The TTS playback worker swallowed its own exceptions, so a bad or under-permissioned ElevenLabs key failed with no feedback. Failures now surface in the Activity field and via NVDA speech.
- **Token-budget truncation now calibrates against real reported usage** instead of a fixed fudge factor — a per-kin real/estimate ratio learned from each call's reported `prompt_tokens`. Streaming calls report usage too now, closing the desktop/room calibration gap. The Usage tab shows the real "most recent send" figure.

## v0.3.4 — 2026-05-21

Docs-only release.

### Added
- **OpenRouter section in `recommended_settings.md`** covering Gemini (2.5 Flash / Flash-Lite / Pro), Claude (Sonnet, Opus, reasoning models), and GPT (4o, 4o-mini, o-series, audio-preview).
- For each provider: recommended sampling settings, thinking-effort guidance, image-token cost notes, audio capability flags, common gotchas (Gemini's stricter safety filters, o-series ignoring temperature, etc.).
- Gemini-specific notes:
  - Temperature scale is 0–2, not 0–1 — temp 1.0 on Gemini ≈ temp 0.8 on most local models.
  - Image cost is a flat 258 tokens/image (vs Anthropic's size-dependent ~1500). For image-heavy budget-conscious chats, switching to Gemini Flash for vision pays off fast.
  - `repeat_penalty` other than 1.0 visibly flattens Gemini output — if migrating a kin from Claude/Sonnet (where Hearthkin's 1.1 default is fine), drop to 1.0 on Gemini.

## v0.3.3 — 2026-05-21

Security + correctness fix for kin cloning.

### Fixed
- **`clone_agent` was a literal `shutil.copytree`** — every file in the source kin's directory ported to the clone, including the Telegram bot token, the entire allow-list of approved Telegram users, the per-user webcam-permission radios, all configured groups, the cron-entry schedule, and the exec allowlist (the list of "remember and auto-approve this command" decisions made for the source kin).

  **Damage modes:**
  - Two bot workers fighting over one Telegram token — long-poll offset semantics mean only one wins each update; the other silently misses messages or sees them duplicated. The user reported "really fucky" breakage from this.
  - The clone happily accepts messages from the original's allow-listed users without you re-vetting them. Trust bypass.
  - Cron entries fire twice (once per kin) when the clone re-syncs schtasks; double-billing, races on shared conversation files.
  - The clone inherits all of the original's pre-approved shell commands — trust assumptions made about kin A silently transfer to kin B.

### Changed
- **`clone_agent` now safe-default resets** the per-deployment surface:
  - Telegram block in `config.json` reset to defaults (bot_token cleared, enabled=false, allow_from empty, all per-user dicts empty, groups empty).
  - `cron_entries` cleared.
  - `exec_allowlist.json` deleted.
  - `voice_history.md` reset to a fresh log with a "Cloned from `<src>` on `<date>`" header line so the clone's voice-swap audit starts clean.
- **Carries over** (the kin's identity + cognition, which is what you actually want from a clone):
  - soul, memory, distill_prompt
  - model + sampling + thinking + cache + image_history_keep + tool_history_keep configs
  - tools allowlist (`tools.json`)
  - conversation history + attachments + memory/journal entries (so the clone remembers the same things and image refs resolve)
- **`clone_agent` return shape**: now returns a list of human-readable reset summaries (or None on failure) so the caller can tell you what got stripped. The "Clone" menu surfaces this in a confirmation dialog AND the status bar — no silent surprises later about "wait, where did my bot config go?"

If you genuinely want to carry the bot config / cron / exec allowlist onto a clone, do it deliberately by editing the cloned config in Settings (or, for the exec allowlist, by re-approving commands as they come up). Safe defaults > convenient defaults for this operation.

## v0.3.2 — 2026-05-21

NVDA-friendly UI on top of the v0.3.1 usage.log. The raw file is grep-friendly but reads as a flat wall to a screen reader; this release adds a structured dialog that does the parsing and aggregation up front.

### Added
- **Tools → Usage history…** opens a new `UsageHistoryDialog`. Tab-reachable sections, top to bottom:
  - **Date range** radio: Today / Last 24 hours / Last 7 days / All time
  - **Kin filter** dropdown (auto-populated from log contents)
  - **Refresh** button (re-reads the file)
  - **Summary** — total calls, tokens in/out, estimated USD spent (with a footnote noting how many calls couldn't be priced — Ollama local or unknown OR model)
  - **By kin / By model / By surface** breakdowns — pre-formatted text blocks sorted by cost descending so the most expensive bucket lands on the eye first
  - **Recent calls** list (last 100, newest first) with timestamp, kin, model, tokens, cost, surface on each line
  - **Open raw log file** button — power-user shortcut into the underlying `usage.log` for those who want the raw grep view
- **`kin_persistence.parse_usage_log()`** — reads up to the last 2000 lines of usage.log into structured dicts. Skips malformed / commented lines silently.
- **`kin_persistence.aggregate_usage(rows, since, kin_filter)`** — sums + groups rows by kin/model/surface, returns the bundle the dialog renders.

### Changed
- **Tools menu**: "Open usage log" renamed to "Open raw usage log" to disambiguate from the new dialog. Both menu items live there now — the dialog is the default for normal use, the raw-file shortcut for power users.

## v0.3.1 — 2026-05-21

Cost-visibility hotfix. The v0.3.0 image-attachments work landed with two cost-related gaps that surfaced when a user's OpenRouter balance dropped $14 in one day with no clear breakdown of where the money went:

1. The chat-tab `% cap` display only counted text characters; images contributed zero, so an image-heavy chat could read 3% while burning real input tokens per turn.
2. No per-call cost log existed. Users had to ask the kin itself (via the `context_status` tool) to introspect, and that didn't catch distillation passes or non-active-kin Telegram traffic.

### Added
- **`~/.hearthkin/logs/usage.log`** — always-on, one line per `llm_backend.chat()` call. Format: `<iso-timestamp> kin=<name> model=<id> in=<prompt_tokens> out=<completion_tokens> est_cost=$X.XXXX surface=<label>`. Surface labels distinguish desktop / desktop-tool / room / room-tool / telegram-dm / telegram-group / telegram-tool / distill / consolidate / cron-subprocess / cron-isolated / mini-chat. Streaming path (desktop 1-on-1 plain chat) doesn't log yet — capturing the provider's final usage frame from the SSE stream is deferred.
- **Tools → Open usage log** menu item, opens `usage.log` in the platform's default text viewer. Tail it, grep it, sum a day's est_cost column.
- **`image_history_keep` per-kin config** (default 2). Only the N most recent image-bearing user turns send their image bytes to the model; older image turns send their caption text only. The kin's own past assistant reply ("I see a cat on a windowsill…") is already in history, so the model's memory of what the image contained survives without re-paying per-image input tokens on every turn. Set in **Settings → Model & generation → Image history kept**.
- **`chat_helpers.estimate_image_tokens(model)`** — provider-aware per-image token cost lookup. Anthropic ~1500, OpenAI ~1100, Google Gemini 258 flat, Meta llama-vision ~1100, Mistral ~900, Qwen ~700, unknown OR provider 1000, Ollama local default 600. Numbers are rough — the estimator's job is "don't lie about how full context is", not "predict cost to the penny" (that's what usage.log is for).
- **`estimate_message_tokens(msg, model)`** — per-message estimator that adds per-attachment image tokens via the provider table. Wired into the chat-tab `% cap` display so image-heavy chats read truthfully.
- **`_estimate_call_cost`** in llm_backend — looks up OpenRouter pricing from the cached catalogue, multiplies through prompt+completion tokens to compute the USD figure that lands in usage.log. Returns None for Ollama (free) and for OR models we can't find in the catalogue.
- **`surface` kwarg** on `llm_backend.chat()` + `run_tool_loop()` — threaded through every blocking-call call site so usage.log says WHERE each billable call came from.

### Fixed
- The `% cap` display was lying about how full context was on image-heavy chats. Now reflects reality.

### How to read this
Open the usage log via **Tools → Open usage log**. The pattern that drove this hotfix was distillation calls accidentally billing against Sonnet 4.5 because the per-kin "distillation model" field defaulted to "(same as chat model)" — Settings → Memory → Distillation model lets you point distillation at a cheaper model (e.g. `openrouter/google/gemini-2.5-flash`). On image-heavy chats, also drop `image_history_keep` to 1 or 0 if cost matters more than letting the kin re-glance at older photos.

## v0.3.0 — 2026-05-20

Substantial new feature set: image attachments and webcam capture.

### Added
